1→"""Context building for review: ReviewContext, shared helpers, heuristic signals."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→from collections import Counter
     7→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→
    10→from .. import utils as _utils_mod
    11→from ..utils import rel, resolve_path, _read_file_text, \
    12→    enable_file_cache, disable_file_cache
    13→
    14→
    15→# ── ReviewContext dataclass ───────────────────────────────────────
    16→
    17→@dataclass
    18→class ReviewContext:
    19→    """Codebase-wide context for contextual file evaluation."""
    20→    naming_vocabulary: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    21→    error_conventions: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    22→    module_patterns: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    23→    import_graph_summary: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    24→    zone_distribution: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    25→    existing_findings: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    26→    codebase_stats: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    27→    sibling_conventions: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    28→    ai_debt_signals: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    29→    auth_patterns: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    30→    error_strategies: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
    31→
    32→
    33→# ── Shared helpers ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    34→
    35→def _abs(filepath: str) -> str:
    36→    """Resolve filepath to absolute using resolve_path."""
    37→    return resolve_path(filepath)
    38→
    39→
    40→def _file_excerpt(filepath: str, max_lines: int = 30) -> str | None:
    41→    """Read first *max_lines* of a file, returning the text or None."""
    42→    content = _read_file_text(_abs(filepath))
    43→    if content is None:
    44→        return None
    45→    lines = content.splitlines(keepends=True)
    46→    if len(lines) <= max_lines:
    47→        return content
    48→    return "".join(lines[:max_lines]) + f"\n... ({len(lines) - max_lines} more lines)"
    49→
    50→
    51→def _dep_graph_lookup(graph: dict, filepath: str) -> dict:
    52→    """Look up a file in the dep graph, trying absolute and relative keys."""
    53→    abs_path = resolve_path(filepath)
    54→    entry = graph.get(abs_path)
    55→    if entry is not None:
    56→        return entry
    57→    # Try relative path
    58→    rpath = rel(filepath)
    59→    entry = graph.get(rpath)
    60→    if entry is not None:
    61→        return entry
    62→    return {}
    63→
    64→
    65→def _importer_count(entry: dict) -> int:
    66→    """Extract importer count from a dep graph entry."""
    67→    importers = entry.get("importers", set())
    68→    if isinstance(importers, set):
    69→        return len(importers)
    70→    return entry.get("importer_count", 0)
    71→
    72→
    73→# ── Regex patterns for code analysis ─────────────────────────────
    74→
    75→_FUNC_NAME_RE = re.compile(
    76→    r"(?:function|def|async\s+def|async\s+function)\s+(\w+)"
    77→)
    78→_CLASS_NAME_RE = re.compile(r"(?:class|interface|type)\s+(\w+)")
    79→
    80→_ERROR_PATTERNS = {
    81→    "try_catch": re.compile(r"\b(?:try\s*\{|try\s*:)"),
    82→    "returns_null": re.compile(r"\breturn\s+(?:null|None|undefined)\b"),
    83→    "result_type": re.compile(r"\b(?:Result|Either|Ok|Err)\b"),
    84→    "throws": re.compile(r"\b(?:throw\s+new|raise\s+\w)"),
    85→}
    86→
    87→_NAME_PREFIX_RE = re.compile(
    88→    r"^(get|set|is|has|can|should|use|create|make|build|parse|format|"
    89→    r"validate|check|find|fetch|load|save|update|delete|remove|add|"
    90→    r"handle|on|init|setup|render|compute|calculate|transform|convert|"
    91→    r"to|from|with|ensure|assert|process|run|do|manage|execute)"
    92→)
    93→
    94→_FROM_IMPORT_RE = re.compile(
    95→    r"^(?:from\s+\S+\s+import\s+(.+)|import\s+(.+))$", re.MULTILINE
    96→)
    97→
    98→
    99→def _extract_imported_names(content: str) -> set[str]:
   100→    """Extract imported symbol names from a file's import statements."""
   101→    names: set[str] = set()
   102→    for m in _FROM_IMPORT_RE.finditer(content):
   103→        raw = m.group(1) or m.group(2)
   104→        if raw is None:
   105→            continue
   106→        for part in raw.split(","):
   107→            part = part.strip()
   108→            if not part:
   109→                continue
   110→            token = part.split()[0]
   111→            if token.startswith("(") or token == "\\":
   112→                continue
   113→            token = token.strip("()")
   114→            if token.isidentifier():
   115→                names.add(token)
   116→    return names
   117→
   118→
   119→# ── Per-file review context builder ──────────────────────────────
   120→
   121→def build_review_context(path: Path, lang, state: dict,
   122→                         files: list[str] | None = None) -> ReviewContext:
   123→    """Gather codebase conventions for contextual evaluation.
   124→
   125→    If *files* is provided, skip file_finder (avoids redundant filesystem walks).
   126→    """
   127→    if files is None:
   128→        files = lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else []
   129→    ctx = ReviewContext()
   130→
   131→    if not files:
   132→        return ctx
   133→
   134→    already_cached = _utils_mod._cache_enabled
   135→    if not already_cached:
   136→        enable_file_cache()
   137→    try:
   138→        return _build_review_context_inner(files, lang, state, ctx)
   139→    finally:
   140→        if not already_cached:
   141→            disable_file_cache()
   142→
   143→
   144→def _build_review_context_inner(files: list[str], lang, state: dict,
   145→                                ctx: ReviewContext) -> ReviewContext:
   146→    """Inner context builder (runs with file cache enabled)."""
   147→    is_ts = lang.name == "typescript"
   148→
   149→    # Pre-read all file contents once (cache will store them)
   150→    file_contents: dict[str, str] = {}
   151→    for filepath in files:
   152→        content = _read_file_text(_abs(filepath))
   153→        if content is not None:
   154→            file_contents[filepath] = content
   155→
   156→    # 1. Naming vocabulary — extract function/class names, count prefixes
   157→    prefix_counter: Counter = Counter()
   158→    total_names = 0
   159→    for content in file_contents.values():
   160→        for name in _FUNC_NAME_RE.findall(content) + _CLASS_NAME_RE.findall(content):
   161→            total_names += 1
   162→            m = _NAME_PREFIX_RE.match(name)
   163→            if m:
   164→                prefix_counter[m.group(1)] += 1
   165→    ctx.naming_vocabulary = {
   166→        "prefixes": dict(prefix_counter.most_common(20)),
   167→        "total_names": total_names,
   168→    }
   169→
   170→    # 2. Error handling conventions — scan for patterns
   171→    error_counts: Counter = Counter()
   172→    for content in file_contents.values():
   173→        for pattern_name, pattern in _ERROR_PATTERNS.items():
   174→            if pattern.search(content):
   175→                error_counts[pattern_name] += 1
   176→    ctx.error_conventions = dict(error_counts)
   177→
   178→    # 3. Module patterns — what each directory typically uses
   179→    dir_patterns: dict[str, Counter] = {}
   180→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   181→        parts = Path(filepath).parts
   182→        if len(parts) < 2:
   183→            continue
   184→        dir_name = parts[-2] + "/"
   185→        counter = dir_patterns.setdefault(dir_name, Counter())
   186→        if is_ts:
   187→            if re.search(r"\bexport\s+default\b", content):
   188→                counter["default_export"] += 1
   189→            if re.search(r"\bexport\s+(?:function|const|class)\b", content):
   190→                counter["named_export"] += 1
   191→        else:
   192→            if re.search(r"\bdef\s+\w+", content):
   193→                counter["functions"] += 1
   194→            if re.search(r"^__all__\s*=", content, re.MULTILINE):
   195→                counter["explicit_api"] += 1
   196→        if re.search(r"\bclass\s+\w+", content):
   197→            counter["class_based"] += 1
   198→    ctx.module_patterns = {
   199→        d: dict(c.most_common(3)) for d, c in dir_patterns.items() if sum(c.values()) >= 3
   200→    }
   201→
   202→    # 4. Import graph summary — top files by importer count
   203→    if lang._dep_graph:
   204→        graph = lang._dep_graph
   205→        importer_counts = {}
   206→        for f, entry in graph.items():
   207→            ic = _importer_count(entry)
   208→            if ic > 0:
   209→                importer_counts[rel(f)] = ic
   210→        top = sorted(importer_counts.items(), key=lambda x: -x[1])[:20]
   211→        ctx.import_graph_summary = {"top_imported": dict(top)}
   212→
   213→    # 5. Zone distribution
   214→    if lang._zone_map is not None:
   215→        ctx.zone_distribution = lang._zone_map.counts()
   216→
   217→    # 6. Existing findings per file (summaries only)
   218→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
   219→    by_file: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   220→    for f in findings.values():
   221→        if f["status"] == "open":
   222→            by_file.setdefault(f["file"], []).append(
   223→                f"{f['detector']}: {f['summary'][:80]}"
   224→            )
   225→    ctx.existing_findings = by_file
   226→
   227→    # 7. Codebase stats
   228→    total_loc = sum(len(c.splitlines()) for c in file_contents.values())
   229→    ctx.codebase_stats = {
   230→        "total_files": len(file_contents),
   231→        "total_loc": total_loc,
   232→        "avg_file_loc": total_loc // len(file_contents) if file_contents else 0,
   233→    }
   234→
   235→    # 8. Sibling function conventions — what naming/patterns neighbors in same dir use
   236→    dir_functions: dict[str, Counter] = {}
   237→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   238→        parts = Path(filepath).parts
   239→        if len(parts) < 2:
   240→            continue
   241→        dir_name = parts[-2] + "/"
   242→        counter = dir_functions.setdefault(dir_name, Counter())
   243→        for name in _FUNC_NAME_RE.findall(content):
   244→            m = _NAME_PREFIX_RE.match(name)
   245→            if m:
   246→                counter[m.group(1)] += 1
   247→    ctx.sibling_conventions = {
   248→        d: dict(c.most_common(5))
   249→        for d, c in dir_functions.items() if sum(c.values()) >= 3
   250→    }
   251→
   252→    # 9. AI debt signals
   253→    ctx.ai_debt_signals = _gather_ai_debt_signals(file_contents)
   254→
   255→    # 10. Auth patterns
   256→    ctx.auth_patterns = _gather_auth_context(file_contents)
   257→
   258→    # 11. Error strategies per file
   259→    strategies: dict[str, str] = {}
   260→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   261→        strat = _classify_error_strategy(content)
   262→        if strat:
   263→            strategies[rel(filepath)] = strat
   264→    ctx.error_strategies = strategies
   265→
   266→    return ctx
   267→
   268→
   269→def _serialize_context(ctx: ReviewContext) -> dict:
   270→    """Convert ReviewContext to a JSON-serializable dict."""
   271→    d = {
   272→        "naming_vocabulary": ctx.naming_vocabulary,
   273→        "error_conventions": ctx.error_conventions,
   274→        "module_patterns": ctx.module_patterns,
   275→        "import_graph_summary": ctx.import_graph_summary,
   276→        "zone_distribution": ctx.zone_distribution,
   277→        "existing_findings": ctx.existing_findings,
   278→        "codebase_stats": ctx.codebase_stats,
   279→        "sibling_conventions": ctx.sibling_conventions,
   280→    }
   281→    if ctx.ai_debt_signals:
   282→        d["ai_debt_signals"] = ctx.ai_debt_signals
   283→    if ctx.auth_patterns:
   284→        d["auth_patterns"] = ctx.auth_patterns
   285→    if ctx.error_strategies:
   286→        d["error_strategies"] = ctx.error_strategies
   287→    return d
   288→
   289→
   290→# ── Heuristic signal gatherers ────────────────────────────────────
   291→
   292→_COMMENT_RE = re.compile(r"^\s*(?:#|//|/\*|\*)")
   293→_LOG_RE = re.compile(
   294→    r"\b(?:console\.(?:log|warn|error|debug|info)|print\(|logging\.(?:debug|info|warning|error))\b"
   295→)
   296→_GUARD_RE = re.compile(
   297→    r"\b(?:if\s*\(\s*\w+\s*(?:===?\s*null|!==?\s*null|===?\s*undefined|!==?\s*undefined)"
   298→    r"|try\s*\{|try\s*:)\b"
   299→)
   300→_FUNC_BODY_RE = re.compile(
   301→    r"(?:def\s+\w+|function\s+\w+|=>\s*\{)", re.MULTILINE
   302→)
   303→
   304→
   305→def _gather_ai_debt_signals(file_contents: dict[str, str]) -> dict:
   306→    """Compute per-file AI-debt heuristic signals.
   307→
   308→    Returns ``{"file_signals": {path: {signal: value}}, "codebase_avg_comment_ratio": float}``.
   309→    Top 20 files by signal count.
   310→    """
   311→    all_ratios: list[float] = []
   312→    file_signals: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}
   313→
   314→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   315→        rpath = rel(filepath)
   316→        lines = content.splitlines()
   317→        if not lines:
   318→            continue
   319→
   320→        total = len(lines)
   321→        comment_lines = sum(1 for ln in lines if _COMMENT_RE.match(ln))
   322→        comment_ratio = comment_lines / total
   323→
   324→        all_ratios.append(comment_ratio)
   325→
   326→        log_count = len(_LOG_RE.findall(content))
   327→        func_count = len(_FUNC_BODY_RE.findall(content))
   328→        log_density = log_count / max(func_count, 1)
   329→
   330→        guard_count = len(_GUARD_RE.findall(content))
   331→        guard_density = guard_count / max(func_count, 1)
   332→
   333→        signals: dict[str, float] = {}
   334→        if comment_ratio > 0.3:
   335→            signals["comment_ratio"] = round(comment_ratio, 2)
   336→        if log_density > 3.0:
   337→            signals["log_density"] = round(log_density, 1)
   338→        if guard_density > 2.0:
   339→            signals["guard_density"] = round(guard_density, 1)
   340→
   341→        if signals:
   342→            file_signals[rpath] = signals
   343→
   344→    # Top 20 by signal count
   345→    top = dict(sorted(file_signals.items(), key=lambda x: -len(x[1]))[:20])
   346→    avg_ratio = sum(all_ratios) / len(all_ratios) if all_ratios else 0.0
   347→
   348→    return {
   349→        "file_signals": top,
   350→        "codebase_avg_comment_ratio": round(avg_ratio, 3),
   351→    }
   352→
   353→
   354→_ROUTE_AUTH_RE = re.compile(
   355→    r"@(?:app|router|api)\.(?:get|post|put|patch|delete|route)\b"
   356→    r"|app\.(?:get|post|put|patch|delete)\("
   357→    r"|export\s+(?:async\s+)?function\s+(?:GET|POST|PUT|PATCH|DELETE)\b"
   358→    r"|@router\.(?:get|post|put|patch|delete)\b",
   359→    re.MULTILINE,
   360→)
   361→_AUTH_DECORATOR_RE = re.compile(
   362→    r"@(?:login_required|require_auth|auth_required|requires_auth|authenticated)\b"
   363→    r"|\brequireAuth\b|\bwithAuth\b|\bgetServerSession\b|\buseAuth\b"
   364→    r"|\brequest\.user\b|\bsession\.user\b|\bgetUser\b",
   365→)
   366→_RLS_TABLE_RE = re.compile(r"CREATE\s+TABLE\s+(?:IF\s+NOT\s+EXISTS\s+)?(\w+)", re.IGNORECASE)
   367→_RLS_ENABLE_RE = re.compile(
   368→    r"ALTER\s+TABLE\s+(\w+)\s+ENABLE\s+ROW\s+LEVEL\s+SECURITY"
   369→    r"|CREATE\s+POLICY\s+\w+\s+ON\s+(\w+)",
   370→    re.IGNORECASE,
   371→)
   372→_SERVICE_ROLE_RE = re.compile(r"service_role|SERVICE_ROLE|serviceRole")
   373→_SUPABASE_CLIENT_RE = re.compile(r"\bcreateClient\b")
   374→
   375→
   376→def _gather_auth_context(file_contents: dict[str, str]) -> dict:
   377→    """Compute auth/RLS context from file contents.
   378→
   379→    Returns route auth coverage, RLS coverage, service role usage, and auth patterns.
   380→    """
   381→    route_auth: dict[str, dict] = {}
   382→    rls_tables: set[str] = set()
   383→    rls_enabled: set[str] = set()
   384→    service_role_files: list[str] = []
   385→    auth_patterns: dict[str, int] = {}
   386→
   387→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   388→        rpath = rel(filepath)
   389→
   390→        # Route auth coverage
   391→        route_matches = _ROUTE_AUTH_RE.findall(content)
   392→        if route_matches:
   393→            auth_matches = _AUTH_DECORATOR_RE.findall(content)
   394→            handler_count = len(route_matches)
   395→            auth_count = len(auth_matches)
   396→            route_auth[rpath] = {
   397→                "handlers": handler_count,
   398→                "with_auth": min(auth_count, handler_count),
   399→                "without_auth": max(0, handler_count - auth_count),
   400→            }
   401→
   402→        # RLS coverage (SQL/migration files)
   403→        for m in _RLS_TABLE_RE.finditer(content):
   404→            rls_tables.add(m.group(1))
   405→        for m in _RLS_ENABLE_RE.finditer(content):
   406→            table = m.group(1) or m.group(2)
   407→            if table:
   408→                rls_enabled.add(table)
   409→
   410→        # Service role usage
   411→        if _SERVICE_ROLE_RE.search(content) and _SUPABASE_CLIENT_RE.search(content):
   412→            service_role_files.append(rpath)
   413→
   414→        # Auth check patterns
   415→        auth_count = len(_AUTH_DECORATOR_RE.findall(content))
   416→        if auth_count > 0:
   417→            auth_patterns[rpath] = auth_count
   418→
   419→    result: dict = {}
   420→    if route_auth:
   421→        result["route_auth_coverage"] = route_auth
   422→    if rls_tables:
   423→        result["rls_coverage"] = {
   424→            "with_rls": sorted(rls_tables & rls_enabled),
   425→            "without_rls": sorted(rls_tables - rls_enabled),
   426→        }
   427→    if service_role_files:
   428→        result["service_role_usage"] = service_role_files
   429→    if auth_patterns:
   430→        result["auth_patterns"] = auth_patterns
   431→    return result
   432→
   433→
   434→_DEPRECATED_RE = re.compile(r"@[Dd]eprecated\b|DEPRECATED", re.MULTILINE)
   435→_MIGRATION_TODO_RE = re.compile(
   436→    r"(?:TODO|FIXME|HACK)\b[^:\n]*\b(?:migrat|legacy|deprecat|old.?api|remove.?after)\b",
   437→    re.IGNORECASE,
   438→)
   439→
   440→# Old+new pattern pairs: (name, old_pattern, new_pattern)
   441→_PATTERN_PAIRS_TS = [
   442→    ("class→functional components", re.compile(r"\bclass\s+\w+\s+extends\s+(?:React\.)?Component\b"),
   443→     re.compile(r"\bfunction\s+\w+\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{.*?return\s*\(?\s*<", re.DOTALL)),
   444→    ("axios→fetch", re.compile(r"\baxios\b"), re.compile(r"\bfetch\(")),
   445→    ("moment→dayjs", re.compile(r"\bmoment\b"), re.compile(r"\bdayjs\b")),
   446→    ("var→let/const", re.compile(r"\bvar\s+\w+"), re.compile(r"\b(?:let|const)\s+\w+")),
   447→    ("require→import", re.compile(r"\brequire\("), re.compile(r"\bimport\s+")),
   448→]
   449→_PATTERN_PAIRS_PY = [
   450→    ("os.path→pathlib", re.compile(r"\bos\.path\b"), re.compile(r"\bpathlib\b|\bPath\(")),
   451→    ("format()→f-string", re.compile(r"\.format\("), re.compile(r'\bf"')),
   452→    ("unittest→pytest", re.compile(r"\bunittest\b"), re.compile(r"\bpytest\b")),
   453→    ("print→logging", re.compile(r"\bprint\("), re.compile(r"\blogging\.\w+\(")),
   454→]
   455→
   456→
   457→def _gather_migration_signals(file_contents: dict[str, str],
   458→                               lang_name: str) -> dict:
   459→    """Compute migration/deprecated signals from file contents.
   460→
   461→    Returns deprecated markers, migration TODOs, pattern pairs, mixed extensions.
   462→    """
   463→    deprecated_files: dict[str, int] = {}
   464→    migration_todos: list[dict] = []
   465→    stems_by_ext: dict[str, set[str]] = {}  # stem -> set of extensions
   466→
   467→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   468→        rpath = rel(filepath)
   469→
   470→        # Deprecated markers
   471→        dep_count = len(_DEPRECATED_RE.findall(content))
   472→        if dep_count > 0:
   473→            deprecated_files[rpath] = dep_count
   474→
   475→        # Migration TODOs
   476→        for m in _MIGRATION_TODO_RE.finditer(content):
   477→            migration_todos.append({"file": rpath, "text": m.group(0)[:120]})
   478→
   479→        # Track stems for mixed extension detection
   480→        p = Path(rpath)
   481→        stem = p.stem
   482→        ext = p.suffix
   483→        if ext in (".js", ".ts", ".jsx", ".tsx"):
   484→            stems_by_ext.setdefault(stem, set()).add(ext)
   485→
   486→    # Pattern pair detection
   487→    pairs = _PATTERN_PAIRS_TS if lang_name == "typescript" else _PATTERN_PAIRS_PY
   488→    pattern_results: list[dict] = []
   489→    for name, old_re, new_re in pairs:
   490→        old_count = sum(1 for c in file_contents.values() if old_re.search(c))
   491→        new_count = sum(1 for c in file_contents.values() if new_re.search(c))
   492→        if old_count > 0 and new_count > 0:
   493→            pattern_results.append({
   494→                "name": name, "old_count": old_count, "new_count": new_count,
   495→            })
   496→
   497→    # Mixed extensions
   498→    mixed_stems = sorted(stem for stem, exts in stems_by_ext.items()
   499→                         if len(exts) >= 2)
   500→
   501→    result: dict = {}
   502→    if deprecated_files:
   503→        result["deprecated_markers"] = {
   504→            "total": sum(deprecated_files.values()),
   505→            "files": deprecated_files,
   506→        }
   507→    if migration_todos:
   508→        result["migration_todos"] = migration_todos[:30]
   509→    if pattern_results:
   510→        result["pattern_pairs"] = pattern_results
   511→    if mixed_stems:
   512→        result["mixed_extensions"] = mixed_stems[:20]
   513→    return result
   514→
   515→
   516→def _classify_error_strategy(content: str) -> str | None:
   517→    """Classify a file's primary error handling strategy."""
   518→    throws = len(re.findall(r"\b(?:throw\s+new|raise\s+\w)", content))
   519→    returns_null = len(re.findall(r"\breturn\s+(?:null|None|undefined)\b", content))
   520→    result_type = len(re.findall(r"\b(?:Result|Either|Ok|Err)\b", content))
   521→    try_catch = len(re.findall(r"\b(?:try\s*\{|try\s*:)", content))
   522→
   523→    counts = {"throw": throws, "return_null": returns_null,
   524→              "result_type": result_type, "try_catch": try_catch}
   525→    total = sum(counts.values())
   526→    if total == 0:
   527→        return None
   528→    dominant = max(counts, key=counts.get)  # type: ignore[arg-type]
   529→    # "mixed" if no strategy accounts for >60% of occurrences
   530→    if counts[dominant] / total < 0.6:
   531→        return "mixed"
   532→    return dominant
   533→
   534→
   535→# ── Holistic context builder ─────────────────────────────────────
   536→
   537→def build_holistic_context(path: Path, lang, state: dict,
   538→                           files: list[str] | None = None) -> dict:
   539→    """Gather codebase-wide data for holistic review across 8 dimensions.
   540→
   541→    Returns a dict with structured data per dimension.
   542→    """
   543→    if files is None:
   544→        files = lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else []
   545→
   546→    already_cached = _utils_mod._cache_enabled
   547→    if not already_cached:
   548→        enable_file_cache()
   549→    try:
   550→        return _build_holistic_context_inner(files, lang, state)
   551→    finally:
   552→        if not already_cached:
   553→            disable_file_cache()
   554→
   555→
   556→def _build_holistic_context_inner(files: list[str], lang, state: dict) -> dict:
   557→    """Inner holistic context builder (runs with file cache enabled)."""
   558→    ctx: dict = {}
   559→
   560→    # Pre-read file contents
   561→    file_contents: dict[str, str] = {}
   562→    for filepath in files:
   563→        content = _read_file_text(_abs(filepath))
   564→        if content is not None:
   565→            file_contents[filepath] = content
   566→
   567→    # 1. Architecture: god modules, dep chains
   568→    arch: dict = {}
   569→    if lang._dep_graph:
   570→        graph = lang._dep_graph
   571→        importer_counts = {}
   572→        for f, entry in graph.items():
   573→            ic = _importer_count(entry)
   574→            if ic > 0:
   575→                importer_counts[rel(f)] = ic
   576→        top_imported = sorted(importer_counts.items(), key=lambda x: -x[1])[:10]
   577→        arch["god_modules"] = [
   578→            {"file": f, "importers": c,
   579→             "excerpt": _file_excerpt(f) or ""}
   580→            for f, c in top_imported if c >= 5
   581→        ]
   582→        arch["top_imported"] = dict(top_imported)
   583→    ctx["architecture"] = arch
   584→
   585→    # 2. Coupling: import-time side effects detection
   586→    coupling: dict = {}
   587→    module_level_io = []
   588→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   589→        lines = content.splitlines()
   590→        for i, line in enumerate(lines[:50]):
   591→            stripped = line.strip()
   592→            if stripped.startswith(("def ", "class ", "async def ", "if ", "#", "@", "import ", "from ")):
   593→                continue
   594→            if re.search(r"\b(?:open|connect|requests?\.|urllib|subprocess|os\.system)\b", stripped):
   595→                module_level_io.append({"file": rel(filepath), "line": i + 1, "code": stripped[:100]})
   596→    if module_level_io:
   597→        coupling["module_level_io"] = module_level_io[:20]
   598→    ctx["coupling"] = coupling
   599→
   600→    # 3. Conventions: naming style per directory
   601→    conventions: dict = {}
   602→    dir_styles: dict[str, Counter] = {}
   603→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   604→        parts = Path(filepath).parts
   605→        if len(parts) < 2:
   606→            continue
   607→        dir_name = parts[-2] + "/"
   608→        counter = dir_styles.setdefault(dir_name, Counter())
   609→        for name in _FUNC_NAME_RE.findall(content):
   610→            if "_" in name and name.islower():
   611→                counter["snake_case"] += 1
   612→            elif name[0].islower() and any(c.isupper() for c in name):
   613→                counter["camelCase"] += 1
   614→            elif name[0].isupper():
   615→                counter["PascalCase"] += 1
   616→    conventions["naming_by_directory"] = {
   617→        d: dict(c.most_common(3)) for d, c in dir_styles.items() if sum(c.values()) >= 3
   618→    }
   619→
   620→    # 3b. Sibling behavior: imports shared across files in same directory
   621→    dir_imports: dict[str, dict[str, set[str]]] = {}
   622→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   623→        parts = Path(filepath).parts
   624→        if len(parts) < 2:
   625→            continue
   626→        dir_name = parts[-2] + "/"
   627→        rpath = rel(filepath)
   628→        names = _extract_imported_names(content)
   629→        dir_imports.setdefault(dir_name, {})[rpath] = names
   630→
   631→    sibling_behavior: dict = {}
   632→    for dir_name, file_names_map in dir_imports.items():
   633→        total = len(file_names_map)
   634→        if total < 3:
   635→            continue
   636→        name_counts: Counter = Counter()
   637→        for names in file_names_map.values():
   638→            for n in names:
   639→                name_counts[n] += 1
   640→        threshold = total * 0.6
   641→        shared = {n: cnt for n, cnt in name_counts.items() if cnt >= threshold}
   642→        if not shared:
   643→            continue
   644→        outliers = []
   645→        for rpath, names in file_names_map.items():
   646→            missing = [n for n in shared if n not in names]
   647→            if missing:
   648→                outliers.append({"file": rpath, "missing": sorted(missing)})
   649→        if outliers:
   650→            sibling_behavior[dir_name] = {
   651→                "shared_patterns": {n: {"count": cnt, "total": total}
   652→                                    for n, cnt in sorted(shared.items(),
   653→                                                         key=lambda x: -x[1])},
   654→                "outliers": sorted(outliers, key=lambda x: len(x["missing"]),
   655→                                   reverse=True),
   656→            }
   657→    conventions["sibling_behavior"] = sibling_behavior
   658→    ctx["conventions"] = conventions
   659→
   660→    # 4. Error handling: strategy distribution per directory
   661→    errors: dict = {}
   662→    dir_errors: dict[str, Counter] = {}
   663→    for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   664→        parts = Path(filepath).parts
   665→        if len(parts) < 2:
   666→            continue
   667→        dir_name = parts[-2] + "/"
   668→        counter = dir_errors.setdefault(dir_name, Counter())
   669→        for pattern_name, pattern in _ERROR_PATTERNS.items():
   670→            matches = pattern.findall(content)
   671→            if matches:
   672→                counter[pattern_name] += len(matches)
   673→    errors["strategy_by_directory"] = {
   674→        d: dict(c.most_common(5)) for d, c in dir_errors.items() if sum(c.values()) >= 2
   675→    }
   676→    ctx["errors"] = errors
   677→
   678→    # 5. Abstractions: util/helper file inventory
   679→    abstractions: dict = {}
   680→    util_files = []
   681→    for filepath in file_contents:
   682→        rpath = rel(filepath)
   683→        basename = Path(rpath).stem.lower()
   684→        if basename in ("utils", "helpers", "util", "helper", "common", "misc"):
   685→            loc = len(file_contents[filepath].splitlines())
   686→            util_files.append({"file": rpath, "loc": loc,
   687→                               "excerpt": _file_excerpt(filepath) or ""})
   688→    abstractions["util_files"] = sorted(util_files, key=lambda x: -x["loc"])[:20]
   689→    ctx["abstractions"] = abstractions
   690→
   691→    # 6. Dependencies: cycles from existing findings
   692→    deps: dict = {}
   693→    cycle_findings = [f for f in state.get("findings", {}).values()
   694→                      if f.get("detector") == "cycles" and f["status"] == "open"]
   695→    if cycle_findings:
   696→        deps["existing_cycles"] = len(cycle_findings)
   697→        deps["cycle_summaries"] = [f["summary"][:120] for f in cycle_findings[:10]]
   698→    ctx["dependencies"] = deps
   699→
   700→    # 7. Testing: coverage gaps
   701→    testing: dict = {}
   702→    if lang._dep_graph:
   703→        tc_findings = {f["file"] for f in state.get("findings", {}).values()
   704→                       if f.get("detector") == "test_coverage" and f["status"] == "open"}
   705→        if tc_findings:
   706→            graph = lang._dep_graph
   707→            critical_untested = []
   708→            for filepath in tc_findings:
   709→                entry = graph.get(resolve_path(filepath), {})
   710→                ic = _importer_count(entry)
   711→                if ic >= 3:
   712→                    critical_untested.append({"file": filepath, "importers": ic})
   713→            testing["critical_untested"] = sorted(critical_untested, key=lambda x: -x["importers"])[:10]
   714→    testing["total_files"] = len(file_contents)
   715→    ctx["testing"] = testing
   716→
   717→    # 8. API surface: export patterns
   718→    api: dict = {}
   719→    is_ts = lang.name == "typescript"
   720→    if is_ts:
   721→        sync_async_mix = []
   722→        for filepath, content in file_contents.items():
   723→            has_sync = bool(re.search(r"\bexport\s+function\s+\w+", content))
   724→            has_async = bool(re.search(r"\bexport\s+async\s+function\s+\w+", content))
   725→            if has_sync and has_async:
   726→                sync_async_mix.append(rel(filepath))
   727→        if sync_async_mix:
   728→            api["sync_async_mix"] = sync_async_mix[:20]
   729→    ctx["api_surface"] = api
   730→
   731→    # 9. Authorization context
   732→    auth_ctx = _gather_auth_context(file_contents)
   733→    if auth_ctx:
   734→        ctx["authorization"] = auth_ctx
   735→
   736→    # 10. AI debt signals
   737→    ai_debt = _gather_ai_debt_signals(file_contents)
   738→    if ai_debt.get("file_signals"):
   739→        ctx["ai_debt_signals"] = ai_debt
   740→
   741→    # 11. Migration signals
   742→    migration = _gather_migration_signals(file_contents, lang.name)
   743→    if migration:
   744→        ctx["migration_signals"] = migration
   745→
   746→    # Codebase stats
   747→    total_loc = sum(len(c.splitlines()) for c in file_contents.values())
   748→    ctx["codebase_stats"] = {
   749→        "total_files": len(file_contents),
   750→        "total_loc": total_loc,
   751→    }
   752→
   753→    return ctx
   754→

    24→    """Normalize a set or list of paths to sorted relative paths (max 10)."""
    25→    if isinstance(s, set):

    30→def prepare_review(path: Path, lang, state: dict, *,
    31→                   max_files: int = 50, max_age_days: int = 30,
    32→                   force_refresh: bool = False,

    35→    """Prepare review data for agent consumption. Returns structured dict.
    36→
    37→    If *files* is provided, skip file_finder (avoids redundant filesystem walks
    38→    when the caller already has the file list, e.g. from _setup_lang).

    41→        lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else []

    43→
    44→    # Enable file cache for entire prepare operation — context building,

    52→                                           force_refresh=force_refresh,
    53→                                           files=all_files)
    54→        file_requests = _build_file_requests(selected, lang, state)
    55→    finally:

    58→    dims = dimensions or DEFAULT_DIMENSIONS
    59→    lang_guide = LANG_GUIDANCE.get(lang.name, {})

    79→def _build_file_requests(files: list[str], lang, state: dict) -> list[dict]:
    80→    """Build per-file review request dicts."""
    81→    file_requests = []
    82→    for filepath in files:
    83→        content = _read_file_text(_abs(filepath))
    84→        if content is None:
    85→            continue
    86→
    87→        rpath = rel(filepath)

    91→
    92→        # Get import neighbors for context

    96→            imports_raw = entry.get("imports", set())
    97→            importers_raw = entry.get("importers", set())
    98→            neighbors = {
    99→                "imports": _rel_list(imports_raw),
   100→                "importers": _rel_list(importers_raw),
   101→                "importer_count": _importer_count(entry),
   102→            }
   103→
   104→        file_requests.append({
   105→            "file": rpath,
   106→            "content": content,
   107→            "zone": zone,

   109→            "neighbors": neighbors,

   111→        })
   112→    return file_requests

   120→    "Cross-reference findings with the sibling_behavior and convention data",

   155→        "lang_guidance": lang_guide,

   165→def _build_investigation_batches(holistic_ctx: dict, lang) -> list[dict]:
   166→    """Derive up to 6 independent, parallelizable investigation batches from context.
   167→
   168→    Each batch groups related dimensions and the files an agent should read.
   169→    Max 15 files per batch, deduplicated. Batches 5-6 only appear when
   170→    authorization/migration context data is non-empty.
   171→    """
   172→    def _collect(sources: list[list[dict]], key: str = "file") -> list[str]:
   173→        """Collect unique file paths from multiple source lists."""
   174→        seen: set[str] = set()
   175→        out: list[str] = []
   176→        for src in sources:
   177→            for item in src:
   178→                f = item.get(key, "")
   179→                if f and f not in seen:
   180→                    seen.add(f)
   181→                    out.append(f)
   182→        return out[:15]
   183→
   184→    arch = holistic_ctx.get("architecture", {})
   185→    coupling = holistic_ctx.get("coupling", {})
   186→    conventions = holistic_ctx.get("conventions", {})
   187→    abstractions = holistic_ctx.get("abstractions", {})

   190→    api = holistic_ctx.get("api_surface", {})
   191→
   192→    # Batch 1: Architecture & Coupling
   193→    god_modules = arch.get("god_modules", [])
   194→    module_io = coupling.get("module_level_io", [])
   195→    batch1_files = _collect([god_modules, module_io])
   196→    batch1 = {
   197→        "name": "Architecture & Coupling",
   198→        "dimensions": ["cross_module_architecture", "initialization_coupling"],
   199→        "files_to_read": batch1_files,
   200→        "why": "god modules, import-time side effects",
   201→    }
   202→
   203→    # Batch 2: Conventions & Errors — sibling behavior outliers
   204→    sibling = conventions.get("sibling_behavior", {})
   205→    outlier_files = []
   206→    for dir_info in sibling.values():
   207→        for o in dir_info.get("outliers", []):
   208→            outlier_files.append({"file": o["file"]})
   209→    # Also include dirs with mixed error strategies (top 5 by count)
   210→    errors = holistic_ctx.get("errors", {})
   211→    error_dirs = errors.get("strategy_by_directory", {})
   212→    mixed_error_files: list[dict] = []
   213→    for dir_name, strategies in error_dirs.items():
   214→        if len(strategies) >= 3:  # Multiple strategies = potential inconsistency
   215→            mixed_error_files.append({"file": dir_name})
   216→    batch2_files = _collect([outlier_files, mixed_error_files])
   217→    batch2 = {
   218→        "name": "Conventions & Errors",
   219→        "dimensions": ["convention_outlier", "error_consistency"],
   220→        "files_to_read": batch2_files,
   221→        "why": "naming drift, behavioral outliers, mixed error strategies",
   222→    }
   223→
   224→    # Batch 3: Abstractions & Dependencies
   225→    util_files = abstractions.get("util_files", [])
   226→    # Files involved in cycles (from dependency summaries)
   227→    cycle_files: list[dict] = []
   228→    for summary in deps.get("cycle_summaries", []):
   229→        for token in summary.split():
   230→            if "/" in token and "." in token:
   231→                cycle_files.append({"file": token.strip(",'\"")})
   232→    batch3_files = _collect([util_files, cycle_files])
   233→    batch3 = {
   234→        "name": "Abstractions & Dependencies",
   235→        "dimensions": ["abstraction_fitness", "dependency_health"],
   236→        "files_to_read": batch3_files,
   237→        "why": "util dumping grounds, dep cycles",
   238→    }
   239→
   240→    # Batch 4: Testing & API
   241→    critical_untested = testing.get("critical_untested", [])
   242→    sync_async = [{"file": f} for f in api.get("sync_async_mix", [])]
   243→    batch4_files = _collect([critical_untested, sync_async])
   244→    batch4 = {
   245→        "name": "Testing & API",
   246→        "dimensions": ["test_strategy", "api_surface_coherence"],
   247→        "files_to_read": batch4_files,
   248→        "why": "critical untested paths, API inconsistency",
   249→    }
   250→
   251→    # Batch 5: Authorization (only when auth context exists)
   252→    auth_ctx = holistic_ctx.get("authorization", {})
   253→    auth_files: list[dict] = []
   254→    # Route files with auth gaps
   255→    for rpath, info in auth_ctx.get("route_auth_coverage", {}).items():
   256→        if info.get("without_auth", 0) > 0:

   258→    # Service role files
   259→    for rpath in auth_ctx.get("service_role_usage", []):

   261→    batch5_files = _collect([auth_files])
   262→    batch5 = {

   265→        "files_to_read": batch5_files,
   266→        "why": "auth gaps, service role usage, RLS coverage",

   268→
   269→    # Batch 6: AI Debt & Migrations (only when signals exist)
   270→    ai_debt = holistic_ctx.get("ai_debt_signals", {})
   271→    migration = holistic_ctx.get("migration_signals", {})

   275→    for entry in migration.get("deprecated_markers", {}).get("files", {}).keys() if isinstance(migration.get("deprecated_markers", {}).get("files"), dict) else []:
   276→        debt_files.append({"file": entry})
   277→    for entry in migration.get("migration_todos", []):

   279→    batch6_files = _collect([debt_files])

   283→        "files_to_read": batch6_files,
   284→        "why": "AI-generated patterns, deprecated markers, migration TODOs",
   285→    }
   286→
   287→    return [b for b in [batch1, batch2, batch3, batch4, batch5, batch6] if b["files_to_read"]]

1→"""Finding import: import_review_findings, import_holistic_findings, assessments."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import hashlib
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from ..state import make_finding, merge_scan, _now
     9→from ..utils import PROJECT_ROOT
    10→from .dimensions import DIMENSION_PROMPTS, HOLISTIC_DIMENSION_PROMPTS
    11→
    12→
    13→# ── Assessment storage ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    14→
    15→def _store_assessments(state: dict, assessments: dict, source: str):
    16→    """Store dimension assessments in state.
    17→
    18→    *assessments*: ``{dim_name: score}`` or ``{dim_name: {score, ...}}``.
    19→    *source*: ``"per_file"`` or ``"holistic"``.
    20→
    21→    Holistic assessments overwrite per-file for the same dimension.
    22→    Per-file assessments don't overwrite holistic.
    23→    """
    24→    store = state.setdefault("review_assessments", {})
    25→    now = _now()
    26→
    27→    for dim_name, value in assessments.items():
    28→        score = value if isinstance(value, (int, float)) else value.get("score", 0)
    29→        score = max(0, min(100, score))
    30→
    31→        existing = store.get(dim_name)
    32→        if existing and existing.get("source") == "holistic" and source == "per_file":
    33→            continue  # Don't overwrite holistic with per-file
    34→
    35→        store[dim_name] = {
    36→            "score": score,
    37→            "source": source,
    38→            "assessed_at": now,
    39→        }
    40→
    41→
    42→def _extract_findings_and_assessments(
    43→    data: list[dict] | dict,
    44→) -> tuple[list[dict], dict | None]:
    45→    """Parse import data, accepting both legacy (list) and new (dict) formats.
    46→
    47→    Legacy: ``[{finding}, ...]``
    48→    New:    ``{"assessments": {...}, "findings": [{finding}, ...]}``
    49→
    50→    Returns ``(findings_list, assessments_or_none)``.
    51→    """
    52→    if isinstance(data, list):
    53→        return data, None
    54→    if isinstance(data, dict):
    55→        return data.get("findings", []), data.get("assessments") or None
    56→    return [], None
    57→
    58→
    59→# ── Per-file finding import ───────────────────────────────────────
    60→
    61→def import_review_findings(findings_data: list[dict] | dict, state: dict,
    62→                           lang_name: str) -> dict:
    63→    """Import agent-produced review findings into state.
    64→
    65→    Accepts either a bare list of findings (legacy) or a dict with
    66→    ``"assessments"`` and ``"findings"`` keys (new format).
    67→
    68→    Validates structure, creates Finding objects, merges into state.
    69→    Returns diff summary.
    70→    """
    71→    findings_list, assessments = _extract_findings_and_assessments(findings_data)
    72→    if assessments:
    73→        _store_assessments(state, assessments, source="per_file")
    74→
    75→    review_findings = []
    76→    skipped: list[dict] = []
    77→    required_fields = ("file", "dimension", "identifier", "summary", "confidence")
    78→    for idx, f in enumerate(findings_list):
    79→        # Validate required fields
    80→        missing = [k for k in required_fields if k not in f]
    81→        if missing:
    82→            skipped.append({"index": idx, "missing": missing,
    83→                            "identifier": f.get("identifier", "<none>")})
    84→            continue
    85→
    86→        # Validate confidence value
    87→        confidence = f.get("confidence", "low")
    88→        if confidence not in ("high", "medium", "low"):
    89→            confidence = "low"
    90→
    91→        # Validate dimension
    92→        dimension = f["dimension"]
    93→        if dimension not in DIMENSION_PROMPTS:
    94→            skipped.append({"index": idx, "missing": [f"invalid dimension: {dimension}"],
    95→                            "identifier": f.get("identifier", "<none>")})
    96→            continue
    97→
    98→        content_hash = hashlib.sha256(f["summary"].encode()).hexdigest()[:8]
    99→        finding = make_finding(
   100→            detector="review",
   101→            file=str(PROJECT_ROOT / f["file"]),  # make_finding calls rel() internally
   102→            name=f"{dimension}::{f['identifier']}::{content_hash}",
   103→            tier=3,  # Always judgment-required
   104→            confidence=confidence,
   105→            summary=f["summary"],
   106→            detail={
   107→                "dimension": dimension,
   108→                "evidence": f.get("evidence", []),
   109→                "suggestion": f.get("suggestion", ""),
   110→                "reasoning": f.get("reasoning", ""),
   111→                "evidence_lines": f.get("evidence_lines", []),
   112→            },
   113→        )
   114→        finding["lang"] = lang_name
   115→        review_findings.append(finding)
   116→
   117→    # Count files evaluated for potentials
   118→    reviewed_files = set(f["file"] for f in findings_list
   119→                         if all(k in f for k in ("file", "dimension", "identifier",
   120→                                                   "summary", "confidence")))
   121→    pots = state.setdefault("potentials", {}).setdefault(lang_name, {})
   122→    pots["review"] = len(reviewed_files)
   123→
   124→    diff = merge_scan(
   125→        state, review_findings,
   126→        lang=lang_name,
   127→    )
   128→
   129→    # Auto-resolve per-file review findings for re-reviewed files that no longer
   130→    # have findings — the reviewer saw the file and found nothing wrong.
   131→    new_ids = {f["id"] for f in review_findings}
   132→    reviewed_files = set(f["file"] for f in findings_list
   133→                         if all(k in f for k in required_fields))
   134→    for fid, f in state.get("findings", {}).items():
   135→        if (f["status"] == "open" and f.get("detector") == "review"
   136→                and not f.get("detail", {}).get("holistic")
   137→                and f.get("file", "") in reviewed_files
   138→                and fid not in new_ids):
   139→            f["status"] = "auto_resolved"
   140→            f["resolved_at"] = _now()
   141→            f["note"] = "not reported in latest per-file re-import"
   142→            diff["auto_resolved"] = diff.get("auto_resolved", 0) + 1
   143→
   144→    # Track skipped findings in diff
   145→    if skipped:
   146→        diff["skipped"] = len(skipped)
   147→        diff["skipped_details"] = skipped
   148→
   149→    # Update review cache
   150→    _update_review_cache(state, findings_list)
   151→
   152→    return diff
   153→
   154→
   155→def _update_review_cache(state: dict, findings_data: list[dict]):
   156→    """Update per-file review cache with timestamps and content hashes."""
   157→    from .selection import _hash_file
   158→
   159→    rc = state.setdefault("review_cache", {})
   160→    file_cache = rc.setdefault("files", {})
   161→    now = _now()
   162→
   163→    reviewed_files = set(f["file"] for f in findings_data
   164→                         if "file" in f)
   165→    for filepath in reviewed_files:
   166→        abs_path = PROJECT_ROOT / filepath
   167→        content_hash = _hash_file(str(abs_path)) if abs_path.exists() else ""
   168→        file_findings = [f for f in findings_data if f.get("file") == filepath]
   169→        file_cache[filepath] = {
   170→            "content_hash": content_hash,
   171→            "reviewed_at": now,
   172→            "finding_count": len(file_findings),
   173→        }
   174→
   175→
   176→# ── Holistic finding import ──────────────────────────────────────
   177→
   178→def import_holistic_findings(findings_data: list[dict] | dict, state: dict,
   179→                              lang_name: str) -> dict:
   180→    """Import holistic (codebase-wide) findings into state.
   181→
   182→    Accepts either a bare list of findings (legacy) or a dict with
   183→    ``"assessments"`` and ``"findings"`` keys (new format).
   184→
   185→    Holistic findings have no `file` field — stored as file="." with
   186→    detail.holistic=True and detail.related_files=[...].
   187→    Returns diff summary.
   188→    """
   189→    from ..scoring import HOLISTIC_POTENTIAL
   190→
   191→    findings_list, assessments = _extract_findings_and_assessments(findings_data)
   192→    if assessments:
   193→        _store_assessments(state, assessments, source="holistic")
   194→
   195→    review_findings = []
   196→    skipped: list[dict] = []
   197→    holistic_required = ("dimension", "identifier", "summary", "confidence")
   198→    for idx, f in enumerate(findings_list):
   199→        # Validate required fields (no 'file' required for holistic)
   200→        missing = [k for k in holistic_required if k not in f]
   201→        if missing:
   202→            skipped.append({"index": idx, "missing": missing,
   203→                            "identifier": f.get("identifier", "<none>")})
   204→            continue
   205→
   206→        confidence = f.get("confidence", "low")
   207→        if confidence not in ("high", "medium", "low"):
   208→            confidence = "low"
   209→
   210→        dimension = f["dimension"]
   211→        if dimension not in HOLISTIC_DIMENSION_PROMPTS:
   212→            skipped.append({"index": idx, "missing": [f"invalid dimension: {dimension}"],
   213→                            "identifier": f.get("identifier", "<none>")})
   214→            continue
   215→
   216→        related_files = f.get("related_files", [])
   217→
   218→        content_hash = hashlib.sha256(f["summary"].encode()).hexdigest()[:8]
   219→        # Use empty string for file — make_finding calls rel("") which returns "."
   220→        finding = make_finding(
   221→            detector="review",
   222→            file="",
   223→            name=f"holistic::{dimension}::{f['identifier']}::{content_hash}",
   224→            tier=3,
   225→            confidence=confidence,
   226→            summary=f["summary"],
   227→            detail={
   228→                "holistic": True,
   229→                "dimension": dimension,
   230→                "related_files": related_files,
   231→                "evidence": f.get("evidence", []),
   232→                "suggestion": f.get("suggestion", ""),
   233→                "reasoning": f.get("reasoning", ""),
   234→            },
   235→        )
   236→        finding["lang"] = lang_name
   237→        review_findings.append(finding)
   238→
   239→    # Add holistic potential (only once — tracked by review_cache.holistic)
   240→    pots = state.setdefault("potentials", {}).setdefault(lang_name, {})
   241→    rc = state.get("review_cache", {})
   242→    if not rc.get("holistic"):
   243→        pots["review"] = HOLISTIC_POTENTIAL
   244→    elif review_findings:
   245→        pots["review"] = pots.get("review", 0) + HOLISTIC_POTENTIAL
   246→
   247→    diff = merge_scan(
   248→        state, review_findings,
   249→        lang=lang_name,
   250→    )
   251→
   252→    # Auto-resolve old holistic findings not in the new import
   253→    new_ids = {f["id"] for f in review_findings}
   254→    for fid, f in state.get("findings", {}).items():
   255→        if (f["status"] == "open" and f.get("detector") == "review"
   256→                and f.get("detail", {}).get("holistic")
   257→                and fid not in new_ids):
   258→            f["status"] = "auto_resolved"
   259→            f["resolved_at"] = _now()
   260→            f["note"] = "not reported in latest holistic re-import"
   261→            diff["auto_resolved"] = diff.get("auto_resolved", 0) + 1
   262→
   263→    # Track skipped findings in diff
   264→    if skipped:
   265→        diff["skipped"] = len(skipped)
   266→        diff["skipped_details"] = skipped
   267→
   268→    _update_holistic_review_cache(state, findings_list)
   269→
   270→    return diff
   271→
   272→
   273→def _update_holistic_review_cache(state: dict, findings_data: list[dict]):
   274→    """Store holistic review metadata in review_cache."""
   275→    rc = state.setdefault("review_cache", {})
   276→    now = _now()
   277→
   278→    # Count valid findings
   279→    valid = [f for f in findings_data
   280→             if all(k in f for k in ("dimension", "identifier", "summary", "confidence"))
   281→             and f["dimension"] in HOLISTIC_DIMENSION_PROMPTS]
   282→
   283→    total_files = 0
   284→    for lang_pots in state.get("potentials", {}).values():
   285→        total_files += sum(v for v in lang_pots.values())
   286→
   287→    rc["holistic"] = {
   288→        "reviewed_at": now,
   289→        "file_count_at_review": total_files,
   290→        "finding_count": len(valid),
   291→    }
   292→

1→"""File selection and staleness tracking for review."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import hashlib
     6→import re
     7→from datetime import datetime, timezone
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→
    10→from ..utils import rel, _read_file_text
    11→from .context import _abs, _dep_graph_lookup, _importer_count
    12→
    13→
    14→# Files with these name patterns have low subjective review value —
    15→# they're mostly declarations (types, constants, enums) not logic.
    16→_LOW_VALUE_NAMES = re.compile(
    17→    r"(?:^|/)(?:types|constants|enums|index)\.[a-z]+$"
    18→    r"|\.d\.ts$"
    19→)
    20→
    21→# Minimum LOC to be worth a review slot
    22→_MIN_REVIEW_LOC = 20
    23→
    24→
    25→def _hash_file(filepath: str) -> str:
    26→    """Compute a content hash for a file."""
    27→    try:
    28→        content = Path(filepath).read_bytes()
    29→        return hashlib.sha256(content).hexdigest()[:16]
    30→    except OSError:
    31→        return ""
    32→
    33→
    34→def select_files_for_review(
    35→    lang, path: Path, state: dict,
    36→    max_files: int = 50, max_age_days: int = 30, force_refresh: bool = False,
    37→    files: list[str] | None = None,
    38→) -> list[str]:
    39→    """Select production files for review, priority-sorted.
    40→
    41→    If *files* is provided, skip file_finder (avoids redundant filesystem walks).
    42→    """
    43→    if files is None:
    44→        files = lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else []
    45→
    46→    cache = state.get("review_cache", {}).get("files", {})
    47→    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    48→    candidates = []
    49→
    50→    for filepath in files:
    51→        rpath = rel(filepath)
    52→
    53→        # Skip non-production files
    54→        if lang._zone_map is not None:
    55→            zone = lang._zone_map.get(filepath)
    56→            if zone.value in ("test", "generated", "vendor"):
    57→                continue
    58→
    59→        # Skip if cached, content unchanged, and not stale
    60→        if not force_refresh:
    61→            entry = cache.get(rpath)
    62→            if entry:
    63→                current_hash = _hash_file(_abs(filepath))
    64→                if current_hash and current_hash == entry.get("content_hash"):
    65→                    reviewed_at = entry.get("reviewed_at", "")
    66→                    if reviewed_at:
    67→                        try:
    68→                            reviewed = datetime.fromisoformat(reviewed_at)
    69→                            age_days = (now - reviewed).days
    70→                            if age_days <= max_age_days:
    71→                                continue  # Still fresh
    72→                        except (ValueError, TypeError):
    73→                            pass  # Can't parse date, treat as stale
    74→
    75→        priority = _compute_review_priority(filepath, lang, state)
    76→        if priority >= 0:  # Negative = filtered out (too small)
    77→            candidates.append((filepath, priority))
    78→
    79→    candidates.sort(key=lambda x: -x[1])
    80→    return [f for f, _ in candidates[:max_files]]
    81→
    82→
    83→def _compute_review_priority(filepath: str, lang, state: dict) -> int:
    84→    """Higher = more important to review.
    85→
    86→    Prioritizes implementation files with high blast radius and existing findings.
    87→    Deprioritizes types/constants files (low subjective review value).
    88→    """
    89→    score = 0
    90→    rpath = rel(filepath)
    91→
    92→    content = _read_file_text(_abs(filepath))
    93→    loc = len(content.splitlines()) if content is not None else 0
    94→
    95→    # Skip tiny files — not enough to review
    96→    if loc < _MIN_REVIEW_LOC:
    97→        return -1
    98→
    99→    # Low-value files: types, constants, enums, index files, .d.ts
   100→    is_low_value = bool(_LOW_VALUE_NAMES.search(rpath))
   101→
   102→    # High blast radius (many importers)
   103→    if lang._dep_graph:
   104→        entry = _dep_graph_lookup(lang._dep_graph, filepath)
   105→        ic = _importer_count(entry)
   106→        if is_low_value:
   107→            score += ic * 2
   108→        else:
   109→            score += ic * 10
   110→
   111→    # Already has programmatic findings (compound value — review will be richer)
   112→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
   113→    n_findings = sum(1 for f in findings.values()
   114→                     if f.get("file") == rpath and f["status"] == "open")
   115→    score += n_findings * 5
   116→
   117→    # Larger files have more to review
   118→    score += loc // 50
   119→
   120→    # Low-value penalty — push toward bottom but don't exclude entirely
   121→    if is_low_value:
   122→        score = score // 3
   123→
   124→    return score
   125→
   126→
   127→def _get_file_findings(state: dict, filepath: str) -> list[dict]:
   128→    """Get existing open findings for a file (summaries for context)."""
   129→    rpath = rel(filepath)
   130→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
   131→    return [
   132→        {"detector": f["detector"], "summary": f["summary"], "id": f["id"]}
   133→        for f in findings.values()
   134→        if f.get("file") == rpath and f["status"] == "open"
   135→    ]
   136→
   137→
   138→def _count_fresh(state: dict, max_age_days: int) -> int:
   139→    """Count files in review cache that are still fresh."""
   140→    cache = state.get("review_cache", {}).get("files", {})
   141→    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
   142→    count = 0
   143→    for entry in cache.values():
   144→        reviewed_at = entry.get("reviewed_at", "")
   145→        if reviewed_at:
   146→            try:
   147→                reviewed = datetime.fromisoformat(reviewed_at)
   148→                if (now - reviewed).days <= max_age_days:
   149→                    count += 1
   150→            except (ValueError, TypeError):
   151→                pass
   152→    return count
   153→
   154→
   155→def _count_stale(state: dict, max_age_days: int) -> int:
   156→    """Count files in review cache that are stale."""
   157→    cache = state.get("review_cache", {}).get("files", {})
   158→    total = len(cache)
   159→    return total - _count_fresh(state, max_age_days)
   160→

1→"""Remediation plan generation from holistic review findings."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from ..state import _now
     8→
     9→
    10→def generate_remediation_plan(state: dict, lang_name: str, *,
    11→                               output_path: Path | None = None) -> str:
    12→    """Generate a prioritized markdown remediation plan from open holistic findings.
    13→
    14→    Computes score impact per finding, sorts by impact, and produces a
    15→    structured document the user can work through.
    16→
    17→    Returns the markdown content. If *output_path* is given, also writes the file.
    18→    """
    19→    from ..scoring import CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS, HOLISTIC_MULTIPLIER
    20→
    21→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
    22→    holistic = [
    23→        (fid, f) for fid, f in findings.items()
    24→        if f["status"] == "open"
    25→        and f.get("detector") == "review"
    26→        and f.get("detail", {}).get("holistic")
    27→    ]
    28→
    29→    if not holistic:
    30→        content = _empty_plan(state, lang_name)
    31→        if output_path:
    32→            from ..utils import safe_write_text
    33→            safe_write_text(output_path, content)
    34→        return content
    35→
    36→    obj_score = state.get("objective_score") or 0
    37→    obj_strict = state.get("objective_strict") or 0
    38→
    39→    # Get review potential for score impact estimation
    40→    potential = 0
    41→    for lang_pots in state.get("potentials", {}).values():
    42→        potential += lang_pots.get("review", 0)
    43→
    44→    # Compute weight and estimated impact per finding
    45→    entries = []
    46→    total_weight = 0.0
    47→    for fid, f in holistic:
    48→        conf = f.get("confidence", "low")
    49→        weight = CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS.get(conf, 0.3) * HOLISTIC_MULTIPLIER
    50→        detail = f.get("detail", {})
    51→        entries.append({
    52→            "id": fid,
    53→            "dimension": detail.get("dimension", "unknown"),
    54→            "summary": f.get("summary", ""),
    55→            "confidence": conf,
    56→            "weight": weight,
    57→            "impact_pts": (weight / potential * 100) if potential > 0 else 0,
    58→            "related_files": detail.get("related_files", []),
    59→            "evidence": detail.get("evidence", []),
    60→            "suggestion": detail.get("suggestion", ""),
    61→            "reasoning": detail.get("reasoning", ""),
    62→        })
    63→        total_weight += weight
    64→
    65→    entries.sort(key=lambda x: -x["weight"])
    66→    total_impact = (total_weight / potential * 100) if potential > 0 else 0
    67→
    68→    lines: list[str] = []
    69→    _w = lines.append
    70→
    71→    _w("# Holistic Review: Remediation Plan\n")
    72→    _w(f"**Generated**: {_now()[:10]}  ")
    73→    _w(f"**Current score**: {obj_score:.1f}/100 (strict: {obj_strict:.1f}/100)  ")
    74→    _w(f"**Open holistic findings**: {len(entries)}  ")
    75→    _w(f"**Estimated improvement**: ~{total_impact:.1f} pts if all addressed\n")
    76→    _w("---\n")
    77→
    78→    _w("## How to use this plan\n")
    79→    _w("1. Work through findings in priority order (highest impact first)")
    80→    _w("2. For each finding, follow the suggested fix steps")
    81→    _w("3. After fixing, run the `resolve` command shown for that finding")
    82→    _w("4. Run `desloppify scan` to update automated findings and score")
    83→    _w("5. To re-evaluate holistic issues, re-run the full cycle:")
    84→    _w(f"   `desloppify --lang {lang_name} review --prepare --holistic --path <src>`")
    85→    _w("   Then have an agent investigate and import — previously addressed")
    86→    _w("   findings auto-resolve if not re-reported.\n")
    87→    _w("---\n")
    88→
    89→    for i, entry in enumerate(entries, 1):
    90→        stars = 3 if entry["weight"] >= 8 else 2 if entry["weight"] >= 5 else 1
    91→        impact_label = "+" * stars
    92→
    93→        _w(f"## Priority {i}: {entry['summary']}\n")
    94→        _w(f"**Dimension**: {entry['dimension'].replace('_', ' ')} | "
    95→           f"**Confidence**: {entry['confidence']} | "
    96→           f"**Impact**: {impact_label} (~{entry['impact_pts']:.1f} pts)\n")
    97→
    98→        if entry["evidence"]:
    99→            _w("### Evidence\n")
   100→            for ev in entry["evidence"]:
   101→                _w(f"- {ev}")
   102→            _w("")
   103→
   104→        if entry["suggestion"]:
   105→            _w("### Suggested fix\n")
   106→            _w(f"{entry['suggestion']}\n")
   107→
   108→        if entry["related_files"]:
   109→            _w("### Files to modify\n")
   110→            for rf in entry["related_files"]:
   111→                _w(f"- `{rf}`")
   112→            _w("")
   113→
   114→        if entry["reasoning"]:

   117→
   118→        _w("### After fixing\n")

   121→        _w("```\n")

   123→

   125→    _w("After addressing findings, re-run the holistic review cycle:\n")

   130→    _w("```\n")

   132→    _w("")

    77→@pytest.fixture
    78→def mock_lang_with_zones(mock_lang):
    79→    """Mock lang with zone map."""
    80→    zone_map = MagicMock()
    81→
    82→    def get_zone(filepath):
    83→        z = MagicMock()
    84→        fname = filepath.split("/")[-1] if "/" in filepath else filepath
    85→        if "__tests__" in filepath or fname.endswith(".test.ts") or fname.startswith("test_"):
    86→            z.value = "test"
    87→        elif "generated" in fname:
    88→            z.value = "generated"
    89→        else:
    90→            z.value = "production"
    91→        return z
    92→
    93→    zone_map.get = get_zone
    94→    zone_map.counts.return_value = {"production": 3, "test": 1}
    95→    mock_lang._zone_map = zone_map
    96→    return mock_lang

   141→class TestBuildReviewContext:
   142→    def test_empty_files(self, mock_lang, empty_state):
   143→        from desloppify.review import build_review_context
   144→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[])
   145→        ctx = build_review_context(Path("/tmp"), mock_lang, empty_state)
   146→        assert ctx.naming_vocabulary == {}
   147→        assert ctx.codebase_stats == {}
   148→
   149→    def test_naming_vocabulary_extraction(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   150→        from desloppify.review import build_review_context
   151→        (tmp_path / "foo.ts").write_text("function getData() {}\ndef setName(): pass\nclass UserService {}")
   152→        (tmp_path / "bar.ts").write_text("function getUser() {}\nasync function handleClick() {}")
   153→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[
   154→            str(tmp_path / "foo.ts"), str(tmp_path / "bar.ts")
   155→        ])
   156→        ctx = build_review_context(tmp_path, mock_lang, empty_state)
   157→        assert ctx.naming_vocabulary["total_names"] == 5
   158→        assert ctx.naming_vocabulary["prefixes"]["get"] == 2
   159→        assert ctx.naming_vocabulary["prefixes"]["set"] == 1
   160→        assert ctx.naming_vocabulary["prefixes"]["handle"] == 1
   161→
   162→    def test_error_convention_detection(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   163→        from desloppify.review import build_review_context
   164→        (tmp_path / "foo.ts").write_text("try { x } catch(e) {}\nreturn null;")
   165→        (tmp_path / "bar.ts").write_text("throw new Error('fail')")
   166→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[
   167→            str(tmp_path / "foo.ts"), str(tmp_path / "bar.ts")
   168→        ])
   169→        ctx = build_review_context(tmp_path, mock_lang, empty_state)
   170→        assert ctx.error_conventions.get("try_catch") == 1
   171→        assert ctx.error_conventions.get("returns_null") == 1
   172→        assert ctx.error_conventions.get("throws") == 1
   173→
   174→    def test_existing_findings_in_context(self, mock_lang, state_with_findings, tmp_path):

   177→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[str(tmp_path / "foo.ts")])
   178→        ctx = build_review_context(tmp_path, mock_lang, state_with_findings)
   179→        assert "src/foo.ts" in ctx.existing_findings

   225→class TestSelectFilesForReview:
   226→    def test_selects_production_files(self, mock_lang_with_zones, empty_state, tmp_path):
   227→        from desloppify.review import select_files_for_review
   228→        # Create real files with enough content to pass min LOC filter
   229→        src = tmp_path / "src"
   230→        src.mkdir()
   231→        (src / "foo.ts").write_text("export function foo() {}\n" * 25)
   232→        (src / "bar.ts").write_text("export function bar() {}\n" * 25)
   233→        tests = src / "__tests__"
   234→        tests.mkdir()
   235→        (tests / "foo.test.ts").write_text("test('x', () => {})\n" * 25)
   236→        foo_path = str(src / "foo.ts")
   237→        bar_path = str(src / "bar.ts")
   238→        test_path = str(tests / "foo.test.ts")
   239→        mock_lang_with_zones.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[
   240→            foo_path, bar_path, test_path,
   241→        ])
   242→        files = select_files_for_review(mock_lang_with_zones, tmp_path, empty_state)
   243→        assert foo_path in files
   244→        assert bar_path in files
   245→        assert test_path not in files

   247→    def test_max_files_limit(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   248→        from desloppify.review import select_files_for_review
   249→        src = tmp_path / "src"
   250→        src.mkdir()
   251→        paths = []
   252→        for i in range(20):
   253→            f = src / f"file{i}.ts"
   254→            f.write_text("export function x() {}\n" * 25)
   255→            paths.append(str(f))
   256→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=paths)
   257→        files = select_files_for_review(mock_lang, tmp_path, empty_state, max_files=5)
   258→        assert len(files) <= 5

   260→    def test_cache_skip_fresh_files(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   261→        from desloppify.review import select_files_for_review, _hash_file
   262→        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   263→        # Create a real file for hashing
   264→        real_file = tmp_path / "cached.ts"
   265→        real_file.write_text("cached content")
   266→        content_hash = _hash_file(str(real_file))
   267→
   268→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[str(real_file)])
   269→        state = dict(empty_state)
   270→        rpath = str(real_file.relative_to(Path.cwd())) if real_file.is_relative_to(Path.cwd()) else str(real_file)
   271→        # We need to patch rel() to return a stable path
   272→        with patch("desloppify.review.selection.rel", return_value="cached.ts"):
   273→            state["review_cache"] = {
   274→                "files": {
   275→                    "cached.ts": {
   276→                        "content_hash": content_hash,
   277→                        "reviewed_at": now,
   278→                        "finding_count": 0,
   279→                    }
   280→                }
   281→            }
   282→            files = select_files_for_review(mock_lang, tmp_path, state, max_age_days=30)
   283→        assert len(files) == 0
   284→
   285→    def test_cache_refresh_stale_files(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   286→        from desloppify.review import select_files_for_review, _hash_file
   287→        old_time = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(days=60)).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   288→        real_file = tmp_path / "stale.ts"
   289→        real_file.write_text("stale content\n" * 25)  # >= _MIN_REVIEW_LOC
   290→        content_hash = _hash_file(str(real_file))
   291→
   292→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[str(real_file)])
   293→        state = dict(empty_state)
   294→        with patch("desloppify.review.selection.rel", return_value="stale.ts"):
   295→            state["review_cache"] = {
   296→                "files": {
   297→                    "stale.ts": {
   298→                        "content_hash": content_hash,
   299→                        "reviewed_at": old_time,
   300→                        "finding_count": 0,
   301→                    }
   302→                }
   303→            }
   304→            files = select_files_for_review(mock_lang, tmp_path, state, max_age_days=30)
   305→        assert len(files) == 1

   351→    def test_priority_ordering_by_importers(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   352→        from desloppify.review import select_files_for_review
   353→        # Create real files so _read_file_text works (need >= _MIN_REVIEW_LOC)
   354→        src = tmp_path / "src"
   355→        src.mkdir()
   356→        (src / "popular.ts").write_text("export function foo() {}\n" * 30)
   357→        (src / "lonely.ts").write_text("export function bar() {}\n" * 30)
   358→        pop_abs = str(src / "popular.ts")
   359→        lon_abs = str(src / "lonely.ts")
   360→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[pop_abs, lon_abs])
   361→        mock_lang._dep_graph = {
   362→            pop_abs: {"importers": {"a", "b", "c", "d", "e"}, "imports": set()},
   363→            lon_abs: {"importers": set(), "imports": set()},
   364→        }
   365→        files = select_files_for_review(mock_lang, tmp_path, empty_state)
   366→        assert files[0] == pop_abs

   371→class TestPrepareReview:
   372→    def test_basic_prepare(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   373→        from desloppify.review import prepare_review
   374→        f = tmp_path / "foo.ts"
   375→        f.write_text("export function getData() { return 42; }\n" * 25)
   376→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[str(f)])
   377→
   378→        data = prepare_review(tmp_path, mock_lang, empty_state)
   379→        assert data["command"] == "review"
   380→        assert data["total_candidates"] == 1
   381→        assert data["dimensions"] == [
   382→            "naming_quality", "comment_quality", "error_consistency",
   383→            "convention_outlier", "abstraction_fitness",
   384→            "logic_clarity", "contract_coherence", "initialization_coupling",
   385→            "logging_quality", "type_safety", "cross_module_architecture",
   386→            "ai_generated_debt", "authorization_coherence",
   387→        ]
   388→        assert "system_prompt" in data
   389→        assert len(data["files"]) == 1
   390→        assert "export function getData() { return 42; }" in data["files"][0]["content"]
   391→
   392→    def test_custom_dimensions(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   393→        from desloppify.review import prepare_review

   398→        data = prepare_review(tmp_path, mock_lang, empty_state,
   399→                              dimensions=["naming_quality", "comment_quality"])

   403→    def test_file_neighbors_included(self, mock_lang, empty_state, tmp_path):
   404→        from desloppify.review import prepare_review
   405→        f = tmp_path / "foo.ts"
   406→        f.write_text("export function bar() {}\n" * 25)
   407→        mock_lang.file_finder = MagicMock(return_value=[str(f)])
   408→        mock_lang._dep_graph = {
   409→            "foo.ts": {"imports": {"bar.ts"}, "importers": {"baz.ts", "qux.ts"}},
   410→        }
   411→
   412→        with patch("desloppify.review.context.rel", return_value="foo.ts"), \
   413→             patch("desloppify.review.selection.rel", return_value="foo.ts"), \
   414→             patch("desloppify.review.prepare.rel", return_value="foo.ts"):
   415→            data = prepare_review(tmp_path, mock_lang, empty_state)
   416→        if data["files"]:
   417→            neighbors = data["files"][0]["neighbors"]
   418→            if neighbors:  # dep graph lookup may not match the patched rel
   419→                assert "imports" in neighbors

   424→class TestImportReviewFindings:
   425→    def test_import_valid_findings(self, empty_state, sample_findings_data):
   426→        from desloppify.review import import_review_findings
   427→        diff = import_review_findings(sample_findings_data, empty_state, "typescript")
   428→        assert diff["new"] == 3
   429→        # Check findings were added to state
   430→        findings = empty_state["findings"]
   431→        assert len(findings) == 3
   432→        # Check finding IDs follow the pattern
   433→        ids = list(findings.keys())
   434→        assert any("naming_quality" in fid for fid in ids)
   435→        assert any("comment_quality" in fid for fid in ids)
   436→        assert any("error_consistency" in fid for fid in ids)
   437→
   438→    def test_import_skips_malformed_findings(self, empty_state):
   439→        from desloppify.review import import_review_findings
   440→        data = [
   441→            {"file": "foo.ts"},  # Missing required fields
   442→            {"dimension": "naming_quality"},  # Missing file
   443→            {  # Valid
   444→                "file": "src/foo.ts",
   445→                "dimension": "naming_quality",
   446→                "identifier": "foo",
   447→                "summary": "test",
   448→                "confidence": "low",
   449→            },
   450→        ]
   451→        diff = import_review_findings(data, empty_state, "typescript")
   452→        assert diff["new"] == 1

   479→    def test_import_updates_review_cache(self, empty_state, sample_findings_data, tmp_path):
   480→        from desloppify.review import import_review_findings
   481→        # Create actual files so hashing works

   484→            (tmp_path / "src" / "foo.ts").write_text("content")
