```
   506→
   507→class TestReviewNeverExpires:
   508→    """When max_age_days=0, reviews should never be considered stale."""
   509→
   510→    def test_per_file_stale_skipped_with_zero(self):
   511→        """Old review with unchanged content should NOT be flagged when max_age_days=0."""
   512→        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmpdir:
   513→            fp = _make_file(tmpdir, "src/widget.ts", lines=40)
   514→            content = open(fp).read()
   515→            content_hash = hashlib.sha256(content.encode()).hexdigest()
   516→
   517→            # rel_path key must match what rel() returns for this filepath
   518→            rpath = os.path.relpath(fp, os.getcwd())
   519→            old_date = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(days=90)).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   520→            cache = {rpath: {"content_hash": content_hash, "reviewed_at": old_date}}
   521→
   522→            entries, _pot = detect_review_coverage(
   523→                [fp], FakeZoneMap(), cache, "typescript", max_age_days=0)
   524→
   525→            # Should have no stale entry
   526→            stale = [e for e in entries if e["name"] == "stale"]
   527→            assert len(stale) == 0
   528→
   529→    def test_holistic_never_expires_with_zero(self):
   530→        from desloppify.detectors.review_coverage import detect_holistic_review_staleness
   531→        old = (datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(days=365)).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   532→        cache = {"holistic": {"reviewed_at": old, "file_count_at_review": 50, "finding_count": 1}}
   533→        entries = detect_holistic_review_staleness(cache, total_files=50, max_age_days=0)
   534→        assert len(entries) == 0
   535→
   536→    def test_holistic_unreviewed_still_flagged_with_zero(self):
   537→        """Even with max_age_days=0, missing holistic review should still be flagged."""
   538→        from desloppify.detectors.review_coverage import detect_holistic_review_staleness
   539→        entries = detect_holistic_review_staleness({}, total_files=50, max_age_days=0)
   540→        assert len(entries) == 1
   541→        assert entries[0]["name"] == "holistic_unreviewed"
```

```
1→"""Objective dimension-based scoring system.
     2→
     3→Groups detectors into dimensions (coherent aspects of code quality),
     4→computes per-dimension pass rates from potentials, and produces a
     5→tier-weighted overall health score.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→from dataclasses import dataclass
    11→
    12→from .enums import Confidence, Tier
    13→from .zones import EXCLUDED_ZONE_VALUES
    14→
    15→
    16→@dataclass
    17→class Dimension:
    18→    name: str
    19→    tier: int
    20→    detectors: list[str]
    21→
    22→
    23→DIMENSIONS = [
    24→    Dimension("Import hygiene",      1, ["unused"]),
    25→    Dimension("Debug cleanliness",   1, ["logs"]),
    26→    Dimension("API surface",         2, ["exports", "deprecated"]),
    27→    Dimension("File health",         3, ["structural"]),
    28→    Dimension("Component design",    3, ["props"]),
    29→    Dimension("Coupling",            3, ["single_use", "coupling"]),
    30→    Dimension("Organization",        3, ["orphaned", "flat_dirs", "naming", "facade", "stale_exclude"]),
    31→    Dimension("Code quality",        3, ["smells", "react", "dict_keys", "global_mutable_config"]),
    32→    Dimension("Duplication",         3, ["dupes"]),
    33→    Dimension("Pattern consistency", 3, ["patterns"]),
    34→    Dimension("Dependency health",   4, ["cycles"]),
    35→    Dimension("Test health",         4, ["test_coverage"]),
    36→    Dimension("Security",            4, ["security"]),
    37→    Dimension("Audit coverage",        4, ["subjective_review"]),
    38→]
    39→
    40→TIER_WEIGHTS = {Tier.AUTO_FIX: 1, Tier.QUICK_FIX: 2, Tier.JUDGMENT: 3, Tier.MAJOR_REFACTOR: 4}
    41→CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS = {Confidence.HIGH: 1.0, Confidence.MEDIUM: 0.7, Confidence.LOW: 0.3}
    42→
    43→# Minimum checks for full dimension weight — below this, weight is dampened
    44→# proportionally. Prevents small-sample dimensions from swinging the overall score.
    45→MIN_SAMPLE = 200
    46→
    47→# Detectors where potential = file count but findings are per-(file, sub-type).
    48→# Per-file weighted failures are capped at 1.0 to match the file-based denominator.
    49→_FILE_BASED_DETECTORS = {"smells", "dict_keys", "test_coverage", "security", "review", "subjective_review"}
    50→
    51→# Zones excluded from scoring (imported from zones.py canonical source)
    52→_EXCLUDED_ZONES = EXCLUDED_ZONE_VALUES
    53→
    54→# Security findings are only excluded in generated/vendor zones (secrets in tests are real risks)
    55→_SECURITY_EXCLUDED_ZONES = {"generated", "vendor"}
    56→
    57→# Holistic review scoring: findings with file="." and detail.holistic=True
    58→# bypass per-file caps and get a 10x weight multiplier.
    59→HOLISTIC_MULTIPLIER = 10.0
    60→HOLISTIC_POTENTIAL = 10
    61→
    62→# Number of checks per assessed review dimension (controls sample dampening).
    63→# Each assessment dimension gets this many checks, making:
    64→#   sample_factor = ASSESSMENT_CHECKS / MIN_SAMPLE
    65→#   effective_weight = tier_weight * sample_factor
    66→# This keeps individual assessment dimensions lightweight, but assessing more
    67→# dimensions increases total assessment weight proportionally.
    68→ASSESSMENT_CHECKS = 10
    69→
    70→# Statuses that count as failures
    71→_LENIENT_FAILURES = {"open"}
    72→_STRICT_FAILURES = {"open", "wontfix"}
    73→
    74→
    75→def merge_potentials(potentials_by_lang: dict) -> dict[str, int]:
    76→    """Sum potentials across languages per detector."""
    77→    merged: dict[str, int] = {}
    78→    for lang_potentials in potentials_by_lang.values():
    79→        for detector, count in lang_potentials.items():
    80→            merged[detector] = merged.get(detector, 0) + count
    81→    return merged
    82→
    83→
    84→def _detector_pass_rate(
    85→    detector: str,
    86→    findings: dict,
    87→    potential: int,
    88→    *,
    89→    strict: bool = False,
    90→) -> tuple[float, int, float]:
    91→    """Pass rate for one detector.
    92→
    93→    Returns (pass_rate, issue_count, weighted_failures).
    94→    Zero potential -> (1.0, 0, 0.0).
    95→
    96→    For file-based detectors (potential = file count, multiple findings per file),
    97→    per-file weighted failures are capped at 1.0 to prevent unit mismatch.
    98→    """
    99→    if potential <= 0:
   100→        return 1.0, 0, 0.0
   101→
   102→    failure_set = _STRICT_FAILURES if strict else _LENIENT_FAILURES
   103→    issue_count = 0
   104→    excluded_zones = _SECURITY_EXCLUDED_ZONES if detector == "security" else _EXCLUDED_ZONES
   105→
   106→    if detector in _FILE_BASED_DETECTORS:
   107→        # Group by file, cap per-file weight at 1.0
   108→        # For test_coverage: use loc_weight from finding detail instead of confidence
   109→        # so large untested files impact the score more than small ones.
   110→        # Holistic findings (file="." + detail.holistic=True) bypass per-file caps
   111→        # and get HOLISTIC_MULTIPLIER weight.
   112→        use_loc_weight = (detector == "test_coverage")
   113→        by_file: dict[str, float] = {}
   114→        file_cap: dict[str, float] = {}  # per-file cap for loc_weight mode
   115→        holistic_sum = 0.0
   116→        for f in findings.values():
   117→            if f.get("detector") != detector:
   118→                continue
   119→            if f.get("zone", "production") in excluded_zones:
   120→                continue
   121→            if f["status"] in failure_set:
   122→                # Holistic findings: no per-file cap, 10x multiplier
   123→                if f.get("file") == "." and f.get("detail", {}).get("holistic"):
   124→                    weight = CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS.get(f.get("confidence", "medium"), 0.7)
   125→                    holistic_sum += weight * HOLISTIC_MULTIPLIER
   126→                    issue_count += 1
   127→                    continue
   128→                if use_loc_weight:
   129→                    weight = f.get("detail", {}).get("loc_weight", 1.0)
   130→                else:
   131→                    weight = CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS.get(f.get("confidence", "medium"), 0.7)
   132→                file_key = f.get("file", "")
   133→                by_file[file_key] = by_file.get(file_key, 0) + weight
   134→                # Track the single-finding weight as the per-file cap
   135→                # (all findings for the same file have the same loc_weight)
   136→                if use_loc_weight and file_key not in file_cap:
   137→                    file_cap[file_key] = weight
   138→                issue_count += 1
   139→        if use_loc_weight:
   140→            # Cap per-file at the file's loc_weight contribution to potential.
   141→            # A file can have multiple findings (e.g. tested by 3 files with issues)
   142→            # but should never contribute more than its potential share.
   143→            weighted_failures = sum(
   144→                min(w, file_cap.get(fk, w)) for fk, w in by_file.items())
   145→        else:
   146→            weighted_failures = sum(min(1.0, w) for w in by_file.values())
   147→        weighted_failures += holistic_sum
   148→    else:
   149→        weighted_failures = 0.0
   150→        for f in findings.values():
   151→            if f.get("detector") != detector:
   152→                continue
   153→            if f.get("zone", "production") in excluded_zones:
   154→                continue
   155→            if f["status"] in failure_set:
   156→                weight = CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS.get(f.get("confidence", "medium"), 0.7)
   157→                weighted_failures += weight
   158→                issue_count += 1
   159→
   160→    pass_rate = max(0.0, (potential - weighted_failures) / potential)
   161→    return pass_rate, issue_count, weighted_failures
   162→
   163→
   164→def compute_dimension_scores(
   165→    findings: dict,
   166→    potentials: dict[str, int],
   167→    *,
   168→    strict: bool = False,
   169→    review_assessments: dict | None = None,
   170→) -> dict[str, dict]:
   171→    """Compute per-dimension scores from findings and potentials.
   172→
   173→    Returns {dimension_name: {"score": float, "checks": int, "issues": int, "detectors": dict}}.
   174→    Dimensions with no active detectors (all potentials = 0 or missing) are excluded.
   175→
   176→    If *review_assessments* is provided, each assessed dimension becomes a
   177→    first-class scoring dimension (tier 4) with score driven by the assessment,
   178→    not by findings.
   179→    """
   180→    results: dict[str, dict] = {}
   181→    failure_set = _STRICT_FAILURES if strict else _LENIENT_FAILURES
   182→
   183→    for dim in DIMENSIONS:
   184→        total_checks = 0
   185→        total_issues = 0
   186→        total_weighted_failures = 0.0
   187→        detector_detail = {}
   188→
   189→        for det in dim.detectors:
   190→            pot = potentials.get(det, 0)
   191→            if pot <= 0:
   192→                continue
   193→            rate, issues, weighted = _detector_pass_rate(
   194→                det, findings, pot, strict=strict)
   195→            total_checks += pot
   196→            total_issues += issues
   197→            total_weighted_failures += weighted
   198→            detector_detail[det] = {
   199→                "potential": pot, "pass_rate": rate,
   200→                "issues": issues, "weighted_failures": weighted,
   201→            }
   202→
   203→        if total_checks <= 0:
   204→            continue
   205→
   206→        # Potential-weighted pass rate: treats the dimension as one big pool
   207→        dim_score = max(0.0, (total_checks - total_weighted_failures) / total_checks) * 100
   208→
   209→        results[dim.name] = {
   210→            "score": round(dim_score, 1),
   211→            "tier": dim.tier,
   212→            "checks": total_checks,
   213→            "issues": total_issues,
   214→            "detectors": detector_detail,
   215→        }
   216→
   217→    # Append assessment dimensions — each one a first-class scoring dimension.
   218→    # Unassessed dimensions default to 0% (same as test coverage when no tests exist).
   219→    from .review import DEFAULT_DIMENSIONS
   220→    assessed = review_assessments or {}
   221→    existing_lower = {k.lower() for k in results}
   222→
   223→    for dim_name in DEFAULT_DIMENSIONS:
   224→        display = dim_name.replace("_", " ").title()
   225→        if display.lower() in existing_lower:
   226→            display = f"{display} (review)"
   227→
   228→        assessment = assessed.get(dim_name)
   229→        score = max(0, min(100, assessment.get("score", 0))) if assessment else 0.0
   230→        pass_rate = score / 100.0
   231→
   232→        # Count open review findings for this dimension
   233→        issue_count = sum(
   234→            1 for f in findings.values()
   235→            if f.get("detector") == "review"
   236→            and f["status"] in failure_set
   237→            and f.get("detail", {}).get("dimension") == dim_name
   238→        )
   239→
   240→        results[display] = {
   241→            "score": round(float(score), 1),
   242→            "tier": 4,
   243→            "checks": ASSESSMENT_CHECKS,
   244→            "issues": issue_count,
   245→            "detectors": {"review_assessment": {
   246→                "potential": ASSESSMENT_CHECKS,
   247→                "pass_rate": round(pass_rate, 4),
   248→                "issues": issue_count,
   249→                "weighted_failures": round(ASSESSMENT_CHECKS * (1 - pass_rate), 4),
   250→            }},
   251→        }
   252→
   253→    # Also include any extra assessed dimensions not in DEFAULT_DIMENSIONS
   254→    for dim_name, assessment in assessed.items():
   255→        if dim_name in DEFAULT_DIMENSIONS:
   256→            continue
   257→        display = dim_name.replace("_", " ").title()
   258→        if display.lower() in existing_lower:
   259→            display = f"{display} (review)"
   260→        score = max(0, min(100, assessment.get("score", 0)))
   261→        pass_rate = score / 100.0
   262→        issue_count = sum(
   263→            1 for f in findings.values()
   264→            if f.get("detector") == "review"
   265→            and f["status"] in failure_set
   266→            and f.get("detail", {}).get("dimension") == dim_name
   267→        )
   268→        results[display] = {
   269→            "score": round(float(score), 1),
   270→            "tier": 4,
   271→            "checks": ASSESSMENT_CHECKS,
   272→            "issues": issue_count,
   273→            "detectors": {"review_assessment": {
   274→                "potential": ASSESSMENT_CHECKS,
   275→                "pass_rate": round(pass_rate, 4),
   276→                "issues": issue_count,
   277→                "weighted_failures": round(ASSESSMENT_CHECKS * (1 - pass_rate), 4),
   278→            }},
   279→        }
   280→
   281→    return results
   282→
   283→
   284→def compute_objective_score(dimension_scores: dict) -> float:
   285→    """Tier-weighted average of dimension scores, dampened by sample size.
   286→
   287→    Dimensions with fewer than MIN_SAMPLE checks get proportionally reduced
   288→    weight, preventing small-sample dimensions from swinging the overall score.
   289→    """
   290→    if not dimension_scores:
   291→        return 100.0
   292→
   293→    weighted_sum = 0.0
   294→    weight_total = 0.0
   295→    for name, data in dimension_scores.items():
   296→        tier = data["tier"]
   297→        w = TIER_WEIGHTS.get(tier, 2)
   298→        # Dampen weight for small-sample dimensions
   299→        sample_factor = min(1.0, data.get("checks", 0) / MIN_SAMPLE)
   300→        effective_weight = w * sample_factor
   301→        weighted_sum += data["score"] * effective_weight
   302→        weight_total += effective_weight
   303→
   304→    if weight_total == 0:
   305→        return 100.0
   306→    return round(weighted_sum / weight_total, 1)
   307→
   308→
   309→def compute_score_impact(
   310→    dimension_scores: dict,
   311→    potentials: dict[str, int],
   312→    detector: str,
   313→    issues_to_fix: int,
   314→) -> float:
   315→    """Estimate score improvement from fixing N issues in a detector.
   316→
   317→    Returns estimated point increase in the objective score.
   318→    """
   319→    # Find which dimension this detector belongs to.
   320→    # Assessment-based dimensions (detector="review_assessment") have no entry
   321→    # in DIMENSIONS — score changes only via re-review, so impact is 0.
   322→    target_dim = None
   323→    for dim in DIMENSIONS:
   324→        if detector in dim.detectors:
   325→            target_dim = dim
   326→            break
   327→    if target_dim is None or target_dim.name not in dimension_scores:
   328→        return 0.0
   329→
   330→    pot = potentials.get(detector, 0)
   331→    if pot <= 0:
   332→        return 0.0
   333→
   334→    dim_data = dimension_scores[target_dim.name]
   335→    old_score = compute_objective_score(dimension_scores)
   336→
   337→    # Simulate fixing: reduce weighted failures by issues_to_fix * avg_weight
   338→    det_data = dim_data["detectors"].get(detector)
   339→    if not det_data:
   340→        return 0.0
   341→
   342→    old_weighted = det_data["weighted_failures"]
   343→    # Assume fixes are high-confidence (weight=1.0) — conservative estimate
   344→    new_weighted = max(0.0, old_weighted - issues_to_fix * 1.0)
   345→
   346→    # Recompute dimension score with potential-weighted averaging
   347→    total_pot = 0
   348→    total_new_wf = 0.0
   349→    for det in target_dim.detectors:
   350→        d = dim_data["detectors"].get(det)
   351→        if not d:
   352→            continue
   353→        total_pot += d["potential"]
   354→        if det == detector:
   355→            total_new_wf += new_weighted
   356→        else:
   357→            total_new_wf += d["weighted_failures"]
   358→    if total_pot <= 0:
   359→        return 0.0
   360→    new_dim_score = max(0.0, (total_pot - total_new_wf) / total_pot) * 100
   361→
   362→    # Recompute overall with the new dimension score
   363→    simulated = {k: dict(v) for k, v in dimension_scores.items()}
   364→    simulated[target_dim.name] = {**dim_data, "score": round(new_dim_score, 1)}
   365→    new_score = compute_objective_score(simulated)
   366→
   367→    return round(new_score - old_score, 1)
   368→
   369→
   370→def get_dimension_for_detector(detector: str) -> Dimension | None:
   371→    """Look up which dimension a detector belongs to."""
   372→    for dim in DIMENSIONS:
   373→        if detector in dim.detectors:
   374→            return dim
   375→    return None
   376→
```

```
   130→
   131→def detector_names() -> list[str]:
   132→    """All registered detector names, sorted."""
   133→    return sorted(DETECTORS.keys())

   141→def detector_tools() -> dict[str, dict]:
   142→    """DETECTOR_TOOLS-shaped dict for narrative.py backward compat."""
```

```
     2→

     9→
    10→def cmd_review(args):
    11→    """Prepare or import subjective code review findings."""
    12→    from ..state import load_state
    13→    from ._helpers import _state_path, _resolve_lang
    14→
    15→    sp = _state_path(args)
    16→    state = load_state(sp)

    18→

    24→    holistic = getattr(args, "holistic", False)

    26→    if import_file:
    27→        _do_import(import_file, state, lang, sp, holistic=holistic)

    29→        _do_prepare(args, state, lang, sp, holistic=holistic)

    32→def _do_prepare(args, state, lang, sp, holistic=False):
    33→    """Prepare mode: compute what needs review, output to query.json."""
    34→    from ._helpers import _write_query
    35→    from ..narrative import compute_narrative
    36→
    37→    path = Path(args.path)
    38→    dims_str = getattr(args, "dimensions", None)
    39→    dimensions = dims_str.split(",") if dims_str else None
    40→
    41→    # Build zone map and dep graph (required for file selection and context)
    42→    # _setup_lang returns the file list so we can pass it through (avoids re-walking)
    43→    found_files = _setup_lang(lang, path, args._config)
    44→
    45→    lang_name = lang.name
    46→    narrative = compute_narrative(state, lang=lang_name, command="review")

    48→    if holistic:

    55→        data["narrative"] = narrative
    56→        _write_query(data)

    60→        if batches:
    61→            print(c(f"\n  Investigation batches (independent — can run in parallel):", "bold"))
    62→            for i, batch in enumerate(batches, 1):
    63→                n_files = len(batch["files_to_read"])
    64→                print(c(f"    {i}. {batch['name']} ({n_files} files) — {batch['why']}", "dim"))
    65→        print(c(f"\n  Workflow:", "bold"))

    67→            print(c(f"    {step_i}. {step}", "dim"))
    68→        print(c(f"\n  \u2192 query.json updated. "
    69→                f"Review codebase, then: desloppify review --import findings.json --holistic", "cyan"))
    70→    else:
    71→        from ..review import prepare_review
    72→        max_files = getattr(args, "max_files", 50)
    73→        max_age_cli = getattr(args, "max_age", None)
    74→        max_age = max_age_cli if max_age_cli is not None else args._config.get("review_max_age_days", 30)
    75→        force_refresh = getattr(args, "refresh", False)

    87→        print(c(f"  Cache: {data['cache_status']['fresh']} fresh, "
    88→                f"{data['cache_status']['stale']} stale, "
    89→                f"{data['cache_status']['new']} new", "dim"))
    90→        print(c(f"  Dimensions: {', '.join(data['dimensions'])}", "dim"))
    91→        print(c(f"\n  \u2192 query.json updated. "
    92→                f"Review files, then: desloppify review --import findings.json", "cyan"))

    94→
    95→def _do_import(import_file, state, lang, sp, holistic=False):
    96→    """Import mode: ingest agent-produced findings."""
    97→    from ..state import save_state
    98→    from ._helpers import _write_query
    99→    from ..narrative import compute_narrative
   100→
   101→    findings_path = Path(import_file)
   102→    if not findings_path.exists():
   103→        print(c(f"  Error: file not found: {import_file}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   104→        sys.exit(1)
   105→
   106→    try:
   107→        findings_data = json.loads(findings_path.read_text())
   108→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError) as e:
   109→        print(c(f"  Error reading findings: {e}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   110→        sys.exit(1)
   111→
   112→    # Accept both legacy (list) and new format (dict with assessments + findings)
   113→    if isinstance(findings_data, dict):
   114→        if "findings" not in findings_data:
   115→            print(c("  Error: findings object must contain a 'findings' key", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   116→            sys.exit(1)
   117→    elif isinstance(findings_data, list):
   118→        # Legacy format: bare array of findings
   119→        findings_data = {"findings": findings_data}
   120→    else:
   121→        print(c("  Error: findings file must contain a JSON array or object", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   122→        sys.exit(1)
   123→
   124→    if holistic:
   125→        from ..review import import_holistic_findings
   126→        diff = import_holistic_findings(findings_data, state, lang.name)
   127→        label = "Holistic review"
   128→    else:
   129→        from ..review import import_review_findings
   130→        diff = import_review_findings(findings_data, state, lang.name)
   131→        label = "Review"
   132→
   133→    save_state(state, sp)
   134→
   135→    lang_name = lang.name
   136→    narrative = compute_narrative(state, lang=lang_name, command="review")
   137→    _write_query({"command": "review", "action": "import", "mode": "holistic" if holistic else "per_file",
   138→                  "diff": diff, "narrative": narrative})
   139→
   140→    print(c(f"\n  {label} imported:", "bold"))
   141→    print(c(f"  +{diff['new']} new findings, "
   142→            f"{diff['auto_resolved']} resolved, "
   143→            f"{diff['reopened']} reopened", "dim"))
   144→
   145→    # Warn about skipped findings so agent can fix their output
   146→    n_skipped = diff.get("skipped", 0)
   147→    if n_skipped > 0:
   148→        print(c(f"\n  \u26a0 {n_skipped} finding(s) skipped (missing required fields):", "yellow"))
   149→        for detail in diff.get("skipped_details", []):
   150→            reasons = detail['missing']
   151→            # Separate actual missing fields from validation errors
   152→            missing_fields = [r for r in reasons if not r.startswith("invalid ")]
   153→            validation_errors = [r for r in reasons if r.startswith("invalid ")]
   154→            parts = []
   155→            if missing_fields:
   156→                parts.append(f"missing {', '.join(missing_fields)}")
   157→            parts.extend(validation_errors)
   158→            print(c(f"    #{detail['index']} ({detail['identifier']}): "
   159→                    f"{'; '.join(parts)}", "yellow"))
   160→
   161→    # Show assessment summary if any were stored
   162→    assessments = state.get("review_assessments", {})
   163→    if assessments:
   164→        parts = [f"{k.replace('_', ' ')} {v['score']}" for k, v in sorted(assessments.items())]
   165→        print(c(f"\n  Assessments: {', '.join(parts)}", "bold"))
   166→
   167→    open_review = [f for f in state["findings"].values()
   168→                   if f["status"] == "open" and f.get("detector") == "review"]
   169→    if open_review:
   170→        print(c(f"\n  {len(open_review)} review finding{'s' if len(open_review) != 1 else ''} open total", "bold"))
   171→        print(c(f"  Run `desloppify issues` to see the work queue", "dim"))
   172→
   173→    obj_score = state.get("objective_score") or 0
   174→    if obj_score:
   175→        print(c(f"\n  Current score: {obj_score:.1f}/100", "dim"))
   176→
   177→
   178→def _setup_lang(lang, path: Path, config: dict) -> list[str]:
   179→    """Build zone map and dep graph for the language config.
   180→
   181→    Returns the file list from file_finder (so callers can reuse it).
   182→    """
   183→    files: list[str] = []
   184→
   185→    # Build zone map
   186→    if lang.zone_rules and lang.file_finder:
   187→        from ..zones import FileZoneMap
   188→        from ..utils import rel
   189→        files = lang.file_finder(path)
   190→        zone_overrides = config.get("zone_overrides") or None
   191→        lang._zone_map = FileZoneMap(files, lang.zone_rules, rel_fn=rel,
   192→                                      overrides=zone_overrides)
   193→
   194→    # Build dep graph
   195→    if lang.build_dep_graph and lang._dep_graph is None:
   196→        try:
   197→            lang._dep_graph = lang.build_dep_graph(path)
   198→        except Exception:
   199→            pass  # Non-fatal — context will just lack dep graph info
   200→
   201→    return files
   202→
```

```
1→"""Review coverage detector — flags production files lacking design review.
     2→
     3→Runs during every scan. Checks the review_cache (persisted in state) to determine
     4→which production files have been reviewed, are stale, or have changed since review.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from datetime import datetime, timezone
    10→
    11→from ..review import _MIN_REVIEW_LOC, _LOW_VALUE_NAMES, _hash_file
    12→from ..utils import rel, _read_file_text, resolve_path
    13→
    14→
    15→def detect_review_coverage(
    16→    files: list[str],
    17→    zone_map,
    18→    review_cache: dict,
    19→    lang_name: str,
    20→    max_age_days: int = 30,
    21→) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
    22→    """Detect production files missing or with stale design reviews.
    23→
    24→    Args:
    25→        files: list of file paths from file_finder
    26→        zone_map: FileZoneMap (or None)
    27→        review_cache: dict of {rel_path: {content_hash, reviewed_at, finding_count}}
    28→        lang_name: "python" or "typescript"
    29→        max_age_days: reviews older than this are flagged as stale
    30→
    31→    Returns:
    32→        (entries, potential) where potential = count of reviewable production files.
    33→    """
    34→    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    35→    entries: list[dict] = []
    36→    potential = 0
    37→
    38→    for filepath in files:
    39→        rpath = rel(filepath)
    40→
    41→        # Skip non-production files
    42→        if zone_map is not None:
    43→            zone = zone_map.get(filepath)
    44→            if zone.value in ("test", "generated", "vendor", "config", "script"):
    45→                continue
    46→
    47→        # Skip low-value files (types, constants, enums, index, .d.ts)
    48→        if _LOW_VALUE_NAMES.search(rpath):
    49→            continue
    50→
    51→        # Skip files below minimum LOC
    52→        abs_path = resolve_path(filepath)
    53→        content = _read_file_text(abs_path)
    54→        if content is None:
    55→            continue
    56→        loc = len(content.splitlines())
    57→        if loc < _MIN_REVIEW_LOC:
    58→            continue
    59→
    60→        potential += 1
    61→
    62→        # Check review cache
    63→        cached = review_cache.get(rpath)
    64→        if cached is None:
    65→            entries.append({
    66→                "file": abs_path,
    67→                "name": "unreviewed",
    68→                "tier": 4,
    69→                "confidence": "low",
    70→                "summary": f"No design review on record — run `desloppify review --prepare`",
    71→                "detail": {"reason": "unreviewed", "loc": loc},
    72→            })
    73→            continue
    74→
    75→        # Check if content changed since review
    76→        current_hash = _hash_file(abs_path)
    77→        if current_hash and current_hash != cached.get("content_hash", ""):
    78→            entries.append({
    79→                "file": abs_path,
    80→                "name": "changed",
    81→                "tier": 4,
    82→                "confidence": "medium",
    83→                "summary": f"File changed since last review — re-review recommended",
    84→                "detail": {"reason": "changed", "loc": loc},
    85→            })
    86→            continue
    87→
    88→        # max_age_days == 0 means "never" — reviews don't expire
    89→        if max_age_days == 0:
    90→            continue
    91→
    92→        # Check if review is stale (age expired)
    93→        reviewed_at = cached.get("reviewed_at", "")
    94→        if reviewed_at:
    95→            try:
    96→                reviewed = datetime.fromisoformat(reviewed_at)
    97→                age_days = (now - reviewed).days
    98→                if age_days > max_age_days:
    99→                    entries.append({
   100→                        "file": abs_path,
   101→                        "name": "stale",
   102→                        "tier": 4,
   103→                        "confidence": "low",
   104→                        "summary": f"Review is stale ({age_days} days old) — re-review recommended",
   105→                        "detail": {"reason": "stale", "age_days": age_days, "loc": loc},
   106→                    })
   107→                    continue
   108→            except (ValueError, TypeError):
   109→                # Can't parse date — treat as stale
   110→                entries.append({
   111→                    "file": abs_path,
   112→                    "name": "stale",
   113→                    "tier": 4,
   114→                    "confidence": "low",
   115→                    "summary": f"Review date unparseable — re-review recommended",
   116→                    "detail": {"reason": "stale", "loc": loc},
   117→                })
   118→                continue
   119→        else:
   120→            # No reviewed_at — treat as unreviewed
   121→            entries.append({
   122→                "file": abs_path,
   123→                "name": "unreviewed",
   124→                "tier": 4,
   125→                "confidence": "low",
   126→                "summary": f"No design review on record — run `desloppify review --prepare`",
   127→                "detail": {"reason": "unreviewed", "loc": loc},
   128→            })
   129→
   130→    return entries, potential
   131→
   132→
   133→def detect_holistic_review_staleness(
   134→    review_cache: dict,
   135→    total_files: int,
   136→    max_age_days: int = 30,
   137→) -> list[dict]:
   138→    """Detect whether a holistic codebase-wide review is needed.
   139→
   140→    Returns 0 or 1 entries:
   141→    - No holistic review on record → holistic_unreviewed
   142→    - Stale (>max_age_days) → holistic_stale
   143→    - File count drifted >20% since review → holistic_stale
   144→    """
   145→    holistic = review_cache.get("holistic")
   146→    if not holistic:
   147→        return [{
   148→            "file": "",
   149→            "name": "holistic_unreviewed",
   150→            "tier": 4,
   151→            "confidence": "low",
   152→            "summary": "No holistic codebase review on record — run `desloppify review --prepare --holistic`",
   153→            "detail": {"reason": "unreviewed"},
   154→        }]
   155→
   156→    # max_age_days == 0 means "never" — holistic reviews don't expire
   157→    if max_age_days == 0:
   158→        return []
   159→
   160→    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
   161→
   162→    # Check age
   163→    reviewed_at = holistic.get("reviewed_at", "")
   164→    if reviewed_at:
   165→        try:
   166→            reviewed = datetime.fromisoformat(reviewed_at)
   167→            age_days = (now - reviewed).days
   168→            if age_days > max_age_days:
   169→                return [{
   170→                    "file": "",
   171→                    "name": "holistic_stale",
   172→                    "tier": 4,
   173→                    "confidence": "low",
   174→                    "summary": f"Holistic review is stale ({age_days} days old) — re-review recommended",
   175→                    "detail": {"reason": "stale", "age_days": age_days},
   176→                }]
   177→        except (ValueError, TypeError):
   178→            return [{
   179→                "file": "",
   180→                "name": "holistic_stale",
   181→                "tier": 4,
   182→                "confidence": "low",
   183→                "summary": "Holistic review date unparseable — re-review recommended",
   184→                "detail": {"reason": "stale"},
   185→            }]
   186→
   187→    # Check file count drift
   188→    file_count_at_review = holistic.get("file_count_at_review", 0)
   189→    if file_count_at_review > 0 and total_files > 0:
   190→        drift = abs(total_files - file_count_at_review) / file_count_at_review
   191→        if drift > 0.20:
   192→            return [{
   193→                "file": "",
   194→                "name": "holistic_stale",
   195→                "tier": 4,
   196→                "confidence": "low",
   197→                "summary": (f"Codebase changed significantly since holistic review "
   198→                            f"({file_count_at_review}→{total_files} files) — re-review recommended"),
   199→                "detail": {"reason": "drift", "old_files": file_count_at_review,
   200→                           "new_files": total_files},
   201→            }]
   202→
   203→    return []
   204→
```

```
   121→def build_review_context(path: Path, lang, state: dict,
   122→                         files: list[str] | None = None) -> ReviewContext:
   123→    """Gather codebase conventions for contextual evaluation.
   124→
   125→    If *files* is provided, skip file_finder (avoids redundant filesystem walks).
   126→    """
   127→    if files is None:
   128→        files = lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else []
   129→    ctx = ReviewContext()
   130→
   131→    if not files:
   132→        return ctx
   133→
   134→    already_cached = _utils_mod._cache_enabled
   135→    if not already_cached:
   136→        enable_file_cache()
   137→    try:
   138→        return _build_review_context_inner(files, lang, state, ctx)
   139→    finally:
   140→        if not already_cached:
   141→            disable_file_cache()
   142→
   143→
   144→def _build_review_context_inner(files: list[str], lang, state: dict,
```

> AGENT

Good - the cache management is reentrant. When called from `prepare_review`, the cache is already enabled, so `build_review_context` sees `already_cached=True` and doesn't disable it when done. This is correct.

# Code Review Report: `desloppify/review/` Package

## CRITICAL Issues

### 1. `prepare_review` and `prepare_holistic_review` are not reentrant with file cache
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/prepare.py`, lines 46-56 and 136-142

Both `prepare_review()` and `prepare_holistic_review()` unconditionally call `enable_file_cache()` and unconditionally call `disable_file_cache()` in the `finally` block. Unlike `build_review_context()` and `build_holistic_context()` (in context.py, lines 134-141 and 546-553), which correctly check `_utils_mod._cache_enabled` before enabling and only disable if they were the ones who enabled it, the prepare functions stomp the caller's cache state.

```python
# prepare.py line 46 - unconditional enable
enable_file_cache()
try:
    ...
finally:
    disable_file_cache()  # line 56 - unconditional disable, stomps caller
```

Compare with the correct pattern in context.py (line 134):
```python
already_cached = _utils_mod._cache_enabled
if not already_cached:
    enable_file_cache()
try:
    ...
finally:
    if not already_cached:
        disable_file_cache()
```

**Impact:** If any caller enables the file cache before calling `prepare_review()` or `prepare_holistic_review()`, the cache will be disabled when the function returns. In practice this does not cause crashes today because the only callers (`_do_prepare` in review_cmd.py) do not pre-enable the cache -- but it violates the MEMORY.md pattern for cache management and would break if calling code changed.

**Fix:** Apply the same `already_cached` guard pattern used in context.py.

---

## MODERATE Issues

### 2. `_update_holistic_review_cache` computes `file_count_at_review` incorrectly
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/import_findings.py`, lines 283-285

```python
total_files = 0
for lang_pots in state.get("potentials", {}).values():
    total_files += sum(v for v in lang_pots.values())
```

This sums **all potentials across all detectors** (e.g., `unused=30 + smells=50 + review=10 = 90`), not the actual **file count**. The result is used by `detect_holistic_review_staleness` for drift detection (comparing `file_count_at_review` to `total_files`). Since the staleness detector receives the *actual* file count as `total_files`, the drift comparison is comparing apples to oranges.

**Impact:** Drift detection in `detect_holistic_review_staleness` will be unreliable. The "file count changed significantly since holistic review" warning may trigger erroneously or not trigger when it should.

**Fix:** Pass the actual file count through to the function, or use `state.get("codebase_stats", {}).get("total_files", 0)` if stored, or simply use `len(files)` from the calling context.

### 3. `import_holistic_findings` potential management is fragile
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/import_findings.py`, lines 240-245

```python
pots = state.setdefault("potentials", {}).setdefault(lang_name, {})
rc = state.get("review_cache", {})
if not rc.get("holistic"):
    pots["review"] = HOLISTIC_POTENTIAL
elif review_findings:
    pots["review"] = pots.get("review", 0) + HOLISTIC_POTENTIAL
```

The first branch sets `pots["review"] = HOLISTIC_POTENTIAL` (overwriting any existing per-file review potential). The second branch *adds* `HOLISTIC_POTENTIAL` to whatever exists. On repeated holistic imports, this keeps incrementing the potential value (`10, 20, 30, ...`), inflating the denominator for the "review" detector's pass rate calculation. This makes the score look worse and worse with each re-import.

**Impact:** Repeated holistic review imports inflate the review potential, diluting the pass rate. The holistic auto-resolve mechanism should compensate for old findings, but the potential denominator grows unbounded.

### 4. `_build_investigation_batches` line 275 is fragile
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/prepare.py`, line 275

```python
for entry in migration.get("deprecated_markers", {}).get("files", {}).keys() if isinstance(migration.get("deprecated_markers", {}).get("files"), dict) else []:
```

This is a deeply nested inline conditional on a single line. While it works for the expected types (dict or absent), if `deprecated_markers` exists but `files` is unexpectedly a list, the `isinstance` check correctly falls through to `[]`. However, the code is hard to read and maintain. A more robust approach would extract the value first.

**Impact:** Low risk of runtime error (the guard works), but high risk of maintenance bugs.

---

## MINOR Issues

### 5. `_serialize_context` omits `ai_debt_signals` when `file_signals` is empty but dict is truthy
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/context.py`, lines 281-287

```python
if ctx.ai_debt_signals:
    d["ai_debt_signals"] = ctx.ai_debt_signals
```

When `ai_debt_signals = {"file_signals": {}, "codebase_avg_comment_ratio": 0.0}`, this dict is truthy (non-empty), so it's included even though there are no actual signals. The test `test_all_conditional_fields_present` explicitly accepts this behavior, but semantically the check should probably be `if ctx.ai_debt_signals.get("file_signals")` to match the behavior in `build_holistic_context` (line 738: `if ai_debt.get("file_signals")`).

**Impact:** Slightly larger output; no functional issue.

### 6. Test name mismatch: `test_do_import_rejects_non_array`
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tests/test_review.py`, line 1437

The test name says "rejects non array" but it actually tests rejection of a dict without a `"findings"` key. This was correct for the pre-dict-format code but is now misleading. Dicts with a `"findings"` key ARE accepted.

**Impact:** Misleading test name only; test behavior is correct.

### 7. Holistic docstring says "8 dimensions" but there are 11
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/review/context.py`, line 539

```python
def build_holistic_context(path: Path, lang, state: dict,
                           files: list[str] | None = None) -> dict:
    """Gather codebase-wide data for holistic review across 8 dimensions.
```

There are now 11 holistic dimensions, not 8. The docstring is stale.

**Impact:** Documentation only.

### 8. `_setup_lang` test uses `args._config` via `MagicMock` but `_do_prepare` test doesn't set it
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tests/test_review.py`, line 1388

In `TestCmdReviewPrepare.test_do_prepare_writes_query_json`, the test patches `_setup_lang` to skip the actual call, so `args._config` is never accessed. But in the real `_do_prepare`, `args._config` is accessed at line 43 and 74. If someone removes the `_setup_lang` patch, the test would fail because MagicMock doesn't set `_config` by default.

**Impact:** Test fragility only.
